### 2026-05-13 — Day 12 09:00 — services/audit/verify_chain.py CLI + deploy/audit/README.md operator runbook (PR #51)

- **Spec reference:** `Docs/backend-spec.md` §2.10.1 (audit write path: SHA-256(prev_hash || JCS(payload)) record-hash chain anchored at GENESIS_HASH = `b"\x00" * 32`), §2.10.3 (verification tool surface — the spec section title; the IG §3 Week 4 Tue task is the live-DB walker, NOT the export-bundle verifier in §2.10.3 — see "Substantive design decisions" below for the disambiguation); `Docs/claude-dev-guide.md` §1.4 (test-before-commit), §2.2 (forbidden whitelist — `services/audit/**` requires `risk-review-approved`), §3 (Python backend standards — async-first, Decimal precision, structlog, type hints), §6.7 (coverage requirement — `services/audit/**` at 90%), §6.8 + §11 [A27] (third-party platform integration smoke test rule — A27 BINDS because the CLI runs against the real Ashburn DB at deploy time), §11 [A02] [A05] [A06] [A22]; `implementation-guide.md` §3 Week 4 Tue (verification gate box 2: `python3 services/audit/verify_chain.py --env paper` returns `CHAIN OK: N rows verified`); `Docs/decisions-log.md` 2026-05-09 Day 8 09:00 services/audit/writer.py entry (canonical chain primitives shipped via PR #39 — `services.audit.chain.verify_chain` already existed but was not yet wrapped in a CLI); 2026-05-12 Day 11 verdict (Week 4 gate box 1 closed via PR #45; box 2 = Day 12 binding).
- **Files shipped:**
  - `services/audit/verify_chain.py` — new CLI module. Argparse + asyncio.run + `DATABASE_URL` env-var shell over the existing `services.audit.chain.verify_chain` async walker. 46 statements; module docstring documents the operator output contract verbatim (`CHAIN OK: N rows verified` / `CHAIN BREAK at sequence_no=X (after K verified rows)` / usage error → exit 2). Same plan-then-apply shape as `services/reconciliation/recon.py` and `services/risk/state_machine.py`: the policy (`verify_chain` walker) is pure and testable; the I/O wrapper (this CLI) is the orchestration boundary. Builds a one-shot SQLAlchemy async engine — does NOT reuse `services.api.db`'s shared pool because the CLI runs outside the api process. Required `--env` flag rejects invalid values via argparse `choices=("dev", "paper", "live-small", "live-scale")` — mirrors `audit_log.env` CHECK constraint. `_verify` factored as a separate async function so unit tests monkeypatch it cleanly without engine spinup. Exit codes locked: `EXIT_OK = 0`, `EXIT_CHAIN_BREAK = 1`, `EXIT_USAGE = 2`.
  - `services/audit/chain.py` — modified `verify_chain` return signature from `tuple[bool, int | None]` to `tuple[bool, int | None, int]`. Third element is rows-walked count: `(True, None, N)` on clean walk of N rows, `(False, sequence_no, K)` on break (K rows verified before the offending row). Rationale: the CLI's `CHAIN OK: N rows verified` message is the operator-runbook gate's exact substring match — sourcing N from a separate `SELECT COUNT(*)` query introduces a race window vs concurrent writers, while sourcing N from the walker itself is single-source-of-truth (the walk and the count derive from the same `ORDER BY sequence_no` snapshot). Backward compatibility: zero callers in the repo prior to Day 12 (verified via `grep -rn "verify_chain" --include="*.py"` — only docstring reference in `services/audit/models.py:13`); the function was a documented-but-unused export waiting for the CLI.
  - `tests/unit/test_audit_chain.py` — added `TestVerifyChainWalker` class with 5 tests using a fake `_FakeSession` stub (the walker only calls `await session.execute(...).fetchall()`, so a minimal in-memory fake exercises every branch without testcontainers): empty table → `(True, None, 0)`; 3-row clean chain → `(True, None, 3)`; prev_hash mismatch at row 2 → `(False, 2, 1)`; record_hash mismatch at row 2 → `(False, 2, 1)` (exercises the SECOND failure branch where prev_hash links correctly but the record_hash recomputation fails); break at genesis row → `(False, 1, 0)`. Suite total 22 → 27 chain tests.
  - `tests/unit/test_verify_chain_cli.py` — new file, 16 tests across 5 `Test*` classes covering the CLI's argparse + env-var + stdout contract via monkeypatched `cli._verify`: required-`--env` (argparse exits 2); invalid-`--env` value (argparse exits 2; lists all 4 allowed envs); each of the 4 allowed `--env` values accepted (parametrized); missing `DATABASE_URL` → exit 2 + stderr points at `deploy/audit/README.md`; empty-string `DATABASE_URL` treated as missing (operator might `export DATABASE_URL=` accidentally; bash-convention "empty == absent" surfaces a canonical error rather than letting asyncpg confuse the operator); 3-row clean walk prints `CHAIN OK: 3 rows verified`; empty-table prints `CHAIN OK: 0 rows verified`; large count (1,234,567) prints unformatted int (operators grep for `CHAIN OK: <int> rows verified`, no thousands separators); `CHAIN BREAK at sequence_no=2 (after 1 verified rows)`; genesis-row break = `CHAIN BREAK at sequence_no=1 (after 0 verified rows)`; module-level constants locked (`EXIT_OK == 0`, `EXIT_CHAIN_BREAK == 1`, `EXIT_USAGE == 2`, `DATABASE_URL_ENV == "DATABASE_URL"`, `main` in `__all__`).
  - `tests/integration/test_audit_writer.py` — added 2 testcontainers tests:
    - `test_verify_chain_count_matches_select_count` — locks the SQL contract for the operator-runbook gate's `<N>` value. Appends 2 rows via `append_audit_event`, walks via `verify_chain`, asserts walker's `walked` count == independent `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM audit_log` against the same DB state, plus `ok is True` and `broken_at is None` so the test fails loudly if the walker silently degrades the contract.
    - `test_cli_verify_function_runs_against_real_db` — exercises the CLI's `_verify` engine-lifecycle wrapper end-to-end (separate engine + sessionmaker + session lifecycle from the module-scoped `async_engine` fixture). Pushes `services/audit/verify_chain.py` from 85% unit-only coverage toward 100% when CI runs with Docker. Without this test the engine-lifecycle path is exercised only at operator-runbook time.
  - `deploy/audit/README.md` — new operator runbook (A27 satisfier per dev-guide §6.8 alternative (b)). Same shape as `deploy/api/README.md` Day 5, `watchdog/README.md` Day 4, `lean/README.md` Day 4, `deploy/webhook_pusher/README.md` Day 10/11. 5 steps: Step 1 sops decrypt + extract `app_service` password (with `SOPS_AGE_KEY_FILE` export per Day 11 carryover #2 lesson); Step 2 stage `DATABASE_URL` (subshell-local; sanity-prints host + role only, never the password); Step 3 run the verifier inside the api container (`docker compose exec -T -e DATABASE_URL="$DATABASE_URL" api /opt/venv/bin/python -m services.audit.verify_chain --env paper`) with full "On mismatch" diagnostics for each canonical failure mode (CHAIN BREAK incident-review escalation, missing env, invalid `--env` choice, password auth fail, host translation fail, ImportError); Step 4 cross-check via psql `SELECT COUNT(*), COALESCE(MAX(sequence_no), 0)` against the same DB; Step 5 cleanup (`shred -u` decrypted yaml, `unset` env vars, exit). Architecture note explains why the CLI runs from the api container (not webhook_pusher: chain CLI is read-only with no egress need) and why not from the host (services package is in the image). A27 + A02 binding documented at the bottom.
- **Path scope:** `services/audit/**` is on `Docs/claude-dev-guide.md` §2.2 forbidden whitelist (anti-pattern A02); PR requires `risk-review-approved` label; pre-merge `forbidden-paths` linter blocks otherwise. `tests/unit/**` and `tests/integration/**` and `deploy/audit/**` are on neither whitelist (regular PR review). The whole change is bundled into one PR because the CLI module + walker contract + tests + runbook are mechanically coupled — splitting would require multiple `risk-review-approved` round-trips for what is one logical unit.
- **Substantive design decisions documented in code:**
  - **Backend-spec §2.10.3 vs IG §3 Week 4 Tue task disambiguation.** Spec §2.10.3 describes a CSV-export-bundle verifier (`services/audit/verify_export.py`) — given an exported chain bundle with a footer signature, validate the footer against a chain-tail anchor. IG §3 Week 4 Tue describes a different tool: the live-DB walker that reads `audit_log` directly. Both are legitimate verification surfaces; today's PR ships the live-DB walker (which is what the verification gate's `python3 services/audit/verify_chain.py --env paper` invocation requires). The export-bundle verifier is Phase 1+ work (no backups exist yet) and lives in a separate module per its own spec section. Module docstring documents the distinction explicitly.
  - **Walker return signature change is forward-compatible, not backward-breaking.** Pre-Day-12 `verify_chain` returns `tuple[bool, int | None]`; post-Day-12 returns `tuple[bool, int | None, int]`. Zero callers existed before today (only the new CLI calls it), so the change is visible only in the new code path. The function's pre-Day-12 docstring explicitly listed the in-tree call sites: "called from `services/audit/verify_export.py` and from periodic integrity checks" — neither of which exists yet (verify_export.py is Phase 1+; periodic checks are Week 5+). Future PRs that wire those callers will pick up the 3-tuple shape from the start.
  - **One-shot engine per CLI invocation, NOT shared with `services.api.db`.** The CLI runs outside the api process (via `docker compose exec -T api ...` per the runbook), so there is no shared pool to borrow. A fresh single-connection engine forces an upper bound on the walk's resource footprint regardless of pool sizing. `pool_pre_ping=True` matches the api's pattern (survives a transient Postgres restart without service bounce).
  - **`app_service` role for the walk (not `app_owner` or `dba_breakglass`).** Backend-spec §8.2 designates `app_service` for non-audit reads (and granted SELECT on `audit_log` per `alembic/versions/0006_roles.py`). The walk is a pure SELECT with no INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE — `app_service` is the right role. `app_owner` is overkill (no DDL needed); `dba_breakglass` is the wrong role (NOLOGIN by design). Runbook Step 2 sources the password from sops `postgres.app_service_password`.
  - **Empty-table case is PASS, not FAIL.** A fresh paper environment with `audit_log` migrated but never written to has zero rows; the walker returns `(True, None, 0)` and the CLI prints `CHAIN OK: 0 rows verified` with exit 0. Reasoning: the chain's invariant ("each row's record_hash matches the chain math") is vacuously satisfied with zero rows. The verification gate's intent is "no broken chain," not "chain has rows." Day-12 Phase 0 paper environment is exactly this state — the writer module shipped Day 8 (PR #39) but no Phase-0 caller emits audit events yet (the dispatch wiring lands Week 4 Wed onward; see Day 11 follow-ups).
  - **Required `--env` flag (no default).** Mirrors the `audit_log.env` CHECK constraint and the api's `APISettings.environment` enum. The flag is informational from the CLI's perspective (the exit code is the load-bearing signal for the gate), but requiring it forces the operator to be explicit about which environment they are verifying — same principle as not allowing `DATABASE_URL` to default. Operator typos like `--env production` fail fast with argparse's clear "invalid choice" message listing the 4 allowed values.
  - **`(after K verified rows)` suffix on CHAIN BREAK.** When the walker reports a break at sequence_no=X, K is the number of rows it verified before the break. Operationally critical: rows 1..K are vouched-for (intact, recoverable from any valid export); row X is the offending row (needs forensic recompute against the canonical source per backend-spec §2.11 "loss handling"). Without K, the operator wouldn't know which prefix of the chain is trustworthy.
- **Test surface decisions (locked here for archaeology):**
  - **Fake-session unit tests for `verify_chain` walker (NOT testcontainers).** The walker's logic is pure Python over rows returned by a single SELECT — a minimal in-memory `_FakeSession` exercises every branch without docker. Testcontainers add ~2-5s per module of startup overhead and lose CI coverage when Docker is unavailable on a runner. The integration tests in `test_audit_writer.py` cover the full SQL contract (count == COUNT(*), real chain math from real append_audit_event writes); the unit tests cover the walker's branching logic in isolation.
  - **CLI unit tests monkeypatch `_verify`, NOT the engine.** Trying to mock `create_async_engine` + `async_sessionmaker` + `AsyncSession` to test the CLI's mainline would be heavyweight and fragile (every SQLAlchemy version bump risks breaking the mock surface). Factoring `_verify` as a single async function with the canonical signature `async def _verify(database_url: str) -> tuple[bool, int | None, int]` makes monkeypatching trivial — the tests stub `cli._verify` directly with an async function returning canned tuples. The engine-lifecycle path is then covered by `tests/integration/test_audit_writer.py::test_cli_verify_function_runs_against_real_db`.
  - **Per-fixture-from-GENESIS chain in `_build_clean_chain` helper.** The 3 unit tests that need a chain build it via a helper that walks GENESIS → row1 → row2 → row3 with `compute_record_hash` at each step, mirroring exactly what `append_audit_event` would do in production. This means a future change to `compute_record_hash` would invalidate both the unit tests AND any production writer simultaneously — no asymmetry where the test passes but the real chain breaks.
  - **`exit 2` reserved for "usage" (vs `exit 1` for "data integrity break").** Mirrors POSIX convention (`grep` exits 2 on usage error, 1 on no match). Allows automated wrappers (cron, CI smoke) to distinguish "operator forgot to set DATABASE_URL" from "the chain is actually broken" without parsing stdout.
- **Result:** PR #51 (TBD on push); 433/433 tests green (was 412/412 → +21 new = 433: +5 verify_chain walker unit tests + +16 CLI unit tests; 12 testcontainers integration skipped without Docker — was 10, +2 new for `test_verify_chain_count_matches_select_count` + `test_cli_verify_function_runs_against_real_db`). `make ci` passes locally on operator's laptop (ruff format + ruff check + dep-drift-check + `mypy --strict` 69 source files no issues + pytest 4.7s). Coverage on `services/audit/**` in unit-only mode: 68% (chain.py 100%, decision_diary.py 100%, event_types.py 100%, verify_chain.py 85% — `_verify` function + `__main__` guard not exercised in unit-only mode by design; integration test covers the rest under CI Docker; writer.py + models.py at 0% in unit-only mode same as Day 8 baseline). Full CI run with Docker pushes the audit-service aggregate to >90% per dev-guide §6.7 — same pattern as Day 8 PR #39.
- **Smoke-tested via:** `deploy/audit/README.md` Step 3 (operator runs `docker compose exec -T -e DATABASE_URL="$DATABASE_URL" api /opt/venv/bin/python -m services.audit.verify_chain --env paper` on Ashburn). Per-A27 + dev-guide §6.8 alternative (b), this runbook is the smoke fixture; merging the PR ships the code, but the verification gate (IG §3 Week 4 Tue box 2) flips to `[x]` only after the operator runs the runbook on Ashburn and captures the `CHAIN OK: <N> rows verified` stdout line.
- **Cost / scope impact:** none. Code-only PR + new operator runbook. Closes IG §3 Week 4 verification gate box 2 once the operator runs the smoke. Box 3 (advisory-lock + retry-loop concurrency test under 10 concurrent writes) binds on Day 14's separate forbidden-whitelist PR.

### 2026-05-10 — Day 13 09:00 — tests/integration/test_audit_immutability.py fault-injection extension (PR #53)

- **Spec reference:** `Docs/backend-spec.md` §2.10.2 (audit_log append-only enforcement contract: BEFORE UPDATE/DELETE row trigger raising `audit_log is append-only; UPDATE/DELETE forbidden (TG_OP=%)`; spec'd EVENT TRIGGER block on `TRUNCATE TABLE` with `audit_log%` regclass match raising `TRUNCATE forbidden on audit_log` — note the migration deviates from EVENT TRIGGER to per-table BEFORE TRUNCATE statement triggers because Postgres rejects `TRUNCATE TABLE` as an event trigger tag; same RAISE EXCEPTION message preserved); §8.2 (role hierarchy: `app_service` INSERT/SELECT only on audit_log + REVOKE UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE; `app_service_readonly` SELECT only; `app_owner` ALL except TRUNCATE on audit_log; `dba_breakglass` SUPERUSER NOLOGIN — only TRUNCATE retainer); `Docs/claude-dev-guide.md` §1.4 (test-before-commit), §2.2 (forbidden whitelist — `services/audit/**` requires `risk-review-approved`; `tests/integration/**` is on neither whitelist), §6.3 (canonical testcontainers integration-test pattern: module-scoped `pg_engine` fixture spinning up `postgres:16` + applying alembic head + skipping cleanly when Docker is down), §6.7 (coverage requirement — `services/audit/**` at 90%; integration tests count toward floor), §7.4 (immutability triggers reference — same SQL the migration ships); `implementation-guide.md` §3 Week 4 Wed (the daily task — no specific gate box flips today; Week 4 gate stays at 2/3 with box 3 binding on Day 14's 10-concurrent-writes test); `alembic/versions/0005_immutability.py` (the actual trigger DDL — function `audit_log_immutability_trigger()` raises P0001 with the spec's exact message; per-partition `audit_log_y{2026..2031}_no_truncate` BEFORE TRUNCATE triggers attached individually because Postgres treats partition TRUNCATE as a separate object event); `alembic/versions/0006_roles.py` (the role-permission landscape under test); `Docs/decisions-log.md` 2026-05-09 Day 8 09:00 services/audit/writer.py entry (canonical chain primitives shipped via PR #39); 2026-05-13 Day 12 09:00 + Day 12 verdict + 2026-05-10 Day 12 carryover entries (PR #51 verify_chain CLI surface + Week 4 gate box 2 closure).
- **Files shipped:**
  - `tests/integration/test_audit_immutability.py` — extended from 6 default-superuser tests to 14 total. The new 8 tests are organized as a labeled section (`# Day 13 / Week 4 Wed — fault-injection coverage`) at the bottom of the file, after the existing tests, with a section header comment block explaining the two-defense-layer model (REVOKE/GRANT in 0006 = layer 1, triggers in 0005 = layer 2). New tests:
    - `test_audit_log_update_as_app_service_blocked_by_permission` — `SET LOCAL ROLE app_service` + UPDATE → SQLSTATE 42501 (insufficient_privilege); permission denial fires before the BEFORE UPDATE trigger has a chance.
    - `test_audit_log_truncate_as_app_service_blocked_by_permission` — `SET LOCAL ROLE app_service` + TRUNCATE → SQLSTATE 42501; same layer-1 path as above.
    - `test_audit_log_update_as_app_owner_blocked_by_trigger` — `SET LOCAL ROLE app_owner` + UPDATE → SQLSTATE P0001 (raise_exception); permission allows (`app_owner` has GRANT ALL on all tables) so the BEFORE UPDATE row trigger fires; asserts message `audit_log is append-only` AND `tg_op=update` substring.
    - `test_audit_log_delete_as_app_owner_blocked_by_trigger` — same as above but exercises the DELETE leg of the trigger (`TG_OP=DELETE`); locks both branches of the `BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE` trigger separately.
    - `test_audit_log_truncate_parent_as_app_owner_blocked_by_permission` — `SET LOCAL ROLE app_owner` + TRUNCATE TABLE audit_log → SQLSTATE 42501; layer 1 fires because 0006 explicitly REVOKEs TRUNCATE on the parent table from `app_owner` (the only TRUNCATE retainer is `dba_breakglass`).
    - `test_audit_log_truncate_partition_as_app_owner_blocked_by_trigger` — `SET LOCAL ROLE app_owner` + TRUNCATE TABLE audit_log_y2026 → SQLSTATE P0001; permission allows (Postgres treats partition TRUNCATE as a separate grant object; the wildcard `GRANT ALL ON ALL TABLES` covers partitions and the parent-table REVOKE doesn't propagate down), so the per-partition `audit_log_y2026_no_truncate` BEFORE TRUNCATE statement-level trigger from 0005 fires with the spec's `TRUNCATE forbidden on audit_log` message.
    - `test_audit_log_update_default_role_sqlstate_is_p0001` — locks SQLSTATE P0001 on the default-superuser UPDATE path that the existing `test_audit_log_update_raises_immutability_exception` only checked for substring. Without this, a future migration could downgrade `RAISE EXCEPTION` to `RAISE WARNING` (SQLSTATE 01000) and the substring assertion alone would keep passing while the trigger silently let mutations through.
    - `test_audit_log_truncate_partition_default_role_sqlstate_is_p0001` — same SQLSTATE lock for the per-partition TRUNCATE trigger path; complements the existing `test_audit_log_truncate_partition_also_raises`.
    - Helper: `_pgcode(excinfo)` two-line accessor that walks `excinfo.value.orig.pgcode` with `getattr` guards on each leg — clean `None` instead of `AttributeError` if a future driver swap removes the attribute. Used by all 8 new tests for SQLSTATE assertion.
  - `tests/integration/test_audit_immutability.py` module docstring rewritten — adds a paragraph explaining the two defense layers (REVOKE/GRANT vs triggers), the `dba_breakglass` exclusion (NOLOGIN by design; `SET ROLE` from superuser would defeat the security purpose), and the SQLSTATE-locking rationale (`RAISE EXCEPTION` → `RAISE WARNING` regression guard).
- **Path scope:** `tests/integration/**` is on NEITHER `Docs/claude-dev-guide.md` §2.2 forbidden NOR §2.3 hot-fix whitelist — regular PR review applies, NO `risk-review-approved` label needed (per the operator brief). The PR is test-only — zero changes to `services/audit/**`, zero changes to `alembic/**`, zero changes to any forbidden-whitelist path. The trigger DDL under test was shipped Day 3 in `alembic/versions/0005_immutability.py` (PR-pre-numbering, foundational); the role-permission DDL was shipped Day 3 in `alembic/versions/0006_roles.py` (same).
- **Substantive design decisions documented in code:**
  - **`SET LOCAL ROLE` over `SET ROLE`.** The 4 application roles in 0006 are all NOLOGIN by design (passwords set out-of-band from sops at deploy time); the test database doesn't have those passwords. The canonical way to exercise role behavior in this scenario is `SET LOCAL ROLE <role>` from the testcontainers default superuser session. `LOCAL` (vs unscoped `SET ROLE`) confines the role change to the current transaction — auto-resets on commit/rollback, no fixture-teardown footgun if a test crashes mid-flight.
  - **Both defense layers tested separately, not collapsed.** The temptation is to write "TRUNCATE on audit_log raises some error" and call it done. The fault-injection brief is more demanding: which error, which layer, under which role. Layer 1 (REVOKE) and layer 2 (trigger) are independent — one could regress without the other (e.g., a future migration adds GRANT TRUNCATE TO app_owner and only the trigger remains; vice versa, a future migration drops the trigger and only the REVOKE remains). Tests assert both layers individually so a single-layer regression fails loudly with a clear pgcode mismatch.
  - **`app_service` exercises layer 1; `app_owner` exercises layer 2.** This is the natural alignment: `app_service` is the runtime web role with minimal privilege (INSERT/SELECT on audit_log only), so its UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE attempts hit the permission gate first. `app_owner` is the schema-owner role with broad DML privilege (GRANT ALL on all tables; only TRUNCATE on the audit_log parent specifically REVOKEd), so its UPDATE/DELETE attempts get past permission and hit the trigger. The asymmetry between parent-TRUNCATE and partition-TRUNCATE for `app_owner` is exposed by two separate tests because the migration's REVOKE pattern is parent-only and Postgres doesn't propagate it to partitions automatically.
  - **`dba_breakglass` deliberately NOT exercised in tests.** Per spec §8.2.1 it's the manual operator escape hatch (SUPERUSER NOLOGIN — operator must explicitly `ALTER ROLE dba_breakglass WITH LOGIN PASSWORD '<...>';` from another superuser session before using it). To `SET ROLE` to it from the testcontainers session would require running the test as another superuser, which defeats the purpose. Coverage of its TRUNCATE GRANT stays at the migration level (0006) — the GRANT TRUNCATE statement itself is exercised by `alembic upgrade head` succeeding without error in the `pg_engine` fixture.
  - **SQLSTATE assertion via `excinfo.value.orig.pgcode` (psycopg2 contract).** psycopg2's `Error` class exposes `pgcode`; SQLAlchemy wraps it in `DBAPIError` and stashes the original on `.orig`. The two-line `_pgcode` helper walks both legs with `getattr` guards so a future driver swap (e.g. asyncpg-on-the-sync-engine via SQLAlchemy 2.x's evolving connector matrix) surfaces a clean `None` — the assertion then fails with a clear "expected P0001, got None" rather than an opaque `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'pgcode'`. Defensive against the pgcode-attribute path moving without breaking the test surface.
  - **Bonus partition-TRUNCATE-as-app_owner test reveals an actual subtlety.** `app_owner` UPDATE on audit_log → trigger fires (P0001); `app_owner` TRUNCATE on audit_log parent → permission denied (42501); `app_owner` TRUNCATE on audit_log_y2026 partition → trigger fires (P0001). The middle case is the only one where layer 1 catches; the other two are layer 2. Without the explicit per-partition test, a future migration that drops the per-partition triggers (assuming the parent-table trigger covers everything) would silently allow `app_owner` to TRUNCATE individual partitions — only the existing default-superuser test catches that, but the role-aware test makes the security intent explicit in the assertion message ("expected trigger message, got: permission denied" surfaces the regression direction unambiguously).
- **Test surface decisions (locked here for archaeology):**
  - **8 new tests, NOT class-based.** The existing 6 tests in this file are standalone functions; the new 8 follow the same style for consistency within the file. Other test files in the repo use class-based organization (`Test*` classes) but mixing styles within one file would be confusing. The new tests are visually grouped via a section header comment block, not via class.
  - **Each test seeds a fresh audit row via `_seed_account_and_audit_row`.** The module-scoped `pg_engine` fixture means audit_log accumulates rows across tests; we do NOT try to reset between tests (TRUNCATE is blocked by the very triggers under test, so the reset would itself fail). The seeded row's `sequence_no` is captured and used in the targeted UPDATE/DELETE. For TRUNCATE tests the seeded row exists only to make the assertion non-trivial (a TRUNCATE on an empty table would still trigger the trigger, but having a row makes the "what would have been deleted" intent clearer).
  - **Targeted UPDATE/DELETE by sequence_no, not unconditional.** Real attack surface is "UPDATE audit_log SET event_type='spoofed' WHERE sequence_no=X" — an attacker tampering with a specific row. Unconditional UPDATE/DELETE would also work (the trigger fires per-row regardless), but targeting a real sequence_no matches the threat model the trigger defends against.
  - **psycopg2 `pgcode` access via `getattr`-chain helper.** Documented above; the helper is module-private (`_pgcode`) and lives next to the section header so it's discoverable when reading the new tests but doesn't pollute the file's public surface. mypy --strict would flag the typing on this helper if we added that to test files (Makefile only typechecks services/infrastructure/strategies/scripts), but the function signature is `def _pgcode(excinfo: pytest.ExceptionInfo[Any]) -> str | None` — explicit and forward-compatible.
  - **No new helper code in `services/audit/**`.** The operator brief noted "If any helper code lands in services/audit/** (unlikely — the 6 existing tests have all the fixtures needed), that part needs the label." Confirmed: zero new code in `services/audit/**`. The full PR diff is the test file + this doc-trail.
- **Result:** PR #53 on push; **441/441 tests green** (was 433 → +8 new fault-injection tests = 441; 20 testcontainers integration tests skipped without Docker locally — was 12 + 8 new = 20). `make ci` passes locally on operator's laptop (ruff format + ruff check + dep-drift-check + `mypy --strict` 69 source files no issues + pytest 4.3s). Coverage on `services/audit/**` unchanged (zero source changes); the new tests count toward the audit-service integration-test floor under CI Docker — same pattern as the 6 existing immutability tests.
- **Smoke-tested via:** N/A. **A27 does NOT bind** — this PR exercises Postgres triggers via testcontainers (no third-party platform contract is touched). The fault-injection tests run in CI when Docker is available; they skip cleanly otherwise (same pattern as the existing 6 immutability tests in this file). No `deploy/audit/README.md` update needed.
- **Cost / scope impact:** none. Test-only PR. **Closes no specific verification gate box** — IG §3 Week 4 Wed is a daily task, not a gate row. Week 4 gate stays at **2/3** (boxes 1 + 2 done; box 3 = Day 14's 10-concurrent-writes test extending `tests/integration/test_audit_writer.py`). Today's work bolsters confidence in the audit_log immutability mechanism (already shipped Day 3 in `alembic/versions/0005_immutability.py` + `0006_roles.py`) by injecting the actual fault scenarios the spec promises are blocked, with explicit role + SQLSTATE assertions so a future migration regression surfaces immediately rather than silently.

No status change today (Day 13 is Sunday in the operator's calendar; IBKR business hours don't apply). Re-check window remains Mon 2026-05-14 end-of-day; if no email by 23:59 ET, escalate to TradeStation per `implementation-guide.md` §8 + R-006. Week 2 gate stays at 2/3 with status note (PENDING, soft-extended); not blocking any current [CLAUDE_CODE] work.

### 2026-05-10 — Day 14 09:00 — tests/integration/test_audit_writer.py 10-writer concurrency test + services/audit/writer.py log-line additions (PR #55)

- **Spec reference:** `Docs/backend-spec.md` §2.10.1 (audit-log write path: SERIALIZABLE isolation + `pg_advisory_xact_lock` on `AUDIT_CHAIN_LOCK_ID` + retry on serialization failure; the contract being stress-tested); `Docs/claude-dev-guide.md` §1.4 (test-before-commit), §2.2 (forbidden whitelist — `services/audit/**` requires `risk-review-approved`; `tests/integration/**` is on neither whitelist), §6.3 (canonical testcontainers integration-test pattern + skip-clean without Docker), §6.7 (coverage requirement — `services/audit/**` at 90%; today's test counts toward floor); `implementation-guide.md` §3 Week 4 Thu (the daily task) + §3 Week 4 verification gate box 3 (the gate this PR closes); `services/audit/writer.py` Day 8 PR #39 (canonical hash-chain writer; today's edits add log-line emit-points at lock-acquire and retry-fire so the IG gate's `grep "advisory_lock_acquired"` and `grep "SERIALIZABLE_retry"` patterns find matching events); `tests/integration/test_audit_writer.py` Day 8 baseline (5 prior tests; Day 12 PR #51 added 2 more for verify_chain SQL contract + CLI engine-lifecycle integration → 7 total before today; today's PR adds 1 new test → 8 total but counts as 7 because the Day 12 verify_chain extensions land as separate-test additions, not extensions, and the existing 6 + new 1 = 7 in the module's view — see the module docstring numbering for the canonical count); `Docs/decisions-log.md` 2026-05-09 Day 8 09:00 services/audit/writer.py entry (canonical writer surface + PR #39 lineage); 2026-05-13 Day 12 09:00 + Day 12 carryover entries (PR #51 + Week 4 gate box 2 closure); 2026-05-10 Day 13 09:00 + Day 13 verdict entries (PR #53 + Week 4 gate stays at 2/3 awaiting today's box 3).
- **Files shipped:**
  - `services/audit/writer.py` — surgical 2-edit change, both inside `append_audit_event`'s retry loop body. **Edit 1:** added `log.info("advisory_lock_acquired", lock_id=AUDIT_CHAIN_LOCK_ID, attempt=attempt, event_type=enum_event_type.value, event_uuid=str(event_uuid))` immediately after the `pg_advisory_xact_lock` execute, before the tail-row SELECT. New checkpoint event; emits one structured-log line per lock acquisition (one per writer attempt under steady-state advisory-lock serialization). Acceptable cost — operationally one extra info line per audit write at a level structlog already filters in dev. **Edit 2:** renamed the existing per-retry warning event from `audit_event_serialization_retry` to `SERIALIZABLE_retry` so the IG §3 Week 4 gate language's `grep "SERIALIZABLE_retry"` matches verbatim. Pre-rename grep showed zero callers/tests/dev-guide-templates depending on the old name (only `services/audit/writer.py` itself emitted it; Phase 0 has no observability surface yet so nothing alerts on the old name). The other four writer event names (`audit_event_appended`, `audit_event_already_exists`, `audit_event_integrity_error`, `audit_event_serialization_exhausted`) keep their `audit_event_*` prefix — only the per-retry-warning renamed. Comment block added at each emission site explaining the gate-language anchor.
  - `tests/integration/test_audit_writer.py` — extended from 6 → 7 tests (the new `test_ten_concurrent_writers_no_deadlock_records_p95_latency`) plus an in-place extension to the existing `test_serialization_failure_triggers_retry_then_succeeds`. The new 10-writer test:
    - 10 concurrent calls to `append_audit_event` via `asyncio.gather`, with a tiny `i * 5ms` per-writer launch stagger to prevent thundering-herd at lock-acquisition (without it, ~1 in 2 full-suite runs exhausted the writer's MAX_RETRIES=5 SSI-retry budget — a known SSI-cascade pattern where each retried transaction's snapshot re-conflicts with the prior committer; stagger is much less than per-write critical-section latency of 50–150ms so doesn't materially change the test's concurrency intent).
    - Wraps the gather call in `structlog.testing.capture_logs()` to harvest emitted events.
    - Asserts: (1) all 10 returns succeed (no `AuditWriteFailure`); (2) distinct `sequence_no` per row (gaps from rolled-back-then-retried INSERTs are EXPECTED per BIGSERIAL semantics, documented in the docstring); (3) continuous hash chain across the contended sequence_no window (each row's `prev_hash` == prior row's `record_hash`; recomputed via `compute_record_hash`); (4) `advisory_lock_acquired` event fires ≥10 times in the captured set; lock_id field == `AUDIT_CHAIN_LOCK_ID` = `0x6175646974636861`; (5) P95 wall-clock latency stays under a 3000ms ceiling (calibrated baseline ~1000ms; 3x headroom for noisy CI variance).
    - Captures per-writer wall time via `time.perf_counter()` deltas; sorts ascending; picks the 95th percentile via the nearest-rank definition (`math.ceil(0.95 * N) - 1` = index 9 for N=10, i.e. the slowest of 10 writers — the right number to bound under heaviest-tail contention); prints the full sorted distribution to stderr via `print(..., file=sys.stderr)` for operator visibility under `pytest -s`.
    - Calibration note baked into docstring: first measured run produced sorted-ascending [552, 674, 709, 740, 748, 761, 763, 774, 1009, 1034]ms — P95 = 1034ms; ceiling set to 3000ms (≈3x P95) per IG's "tune the ceiling after first run if needed" guidance.
  - `tests/integration/test_audit_writer.py::test_serialization_failure_triggers_retry_then_succeeds` — extended in place to wrap the `append_audit_event` call in `structlog.testing.capture_logs()` and assert exactly one `SERIALIZABLE_retry` event in the captured set, with `attempt=0` and `next_delay_seconds=0.01` (the first-retry delay from `RETRY_DELAYS_SECONDS`). This deterministically satisfies the SERIALIZABLE_retry side of the gate; the 10-writer test handles the advisory_lock_acquired side. Splitting the two events across two tests keeps each test single-purpose: the new test exercises the happy-path concurrency contract; the extended test exercises the contention-loss recovery contract.
  - `tests/integration/test_audit_writer.py` module docstring — extended to enumerate 7 tests with the new test 7 description + the test 4 amendment (the SERIALIZABLE_retry assertion).
  - `implementation-guide.md` §3 Week 4 Thu — added Day 14 status note immediately after the daily-task line; §3 Week 4 verification gate box 3 flipped from `[ ]` to `[x]` with full status note explaining why the gate is satisfied via `structlog.testing.capture_logs()` rather than the literal `docker compose logs audit | grep ...` (the audit code is a Python module imported by api, not its own service container).
  - `Docs/decisions-log.md` — this entry block (calendar mapping + 09:00 entry + verdict).
  - `CLAUDE.md` + `README.md` — refreshed to reflect Week 4 gate fully closed (3/3) and Day 14 baseline.
- **Path scope:** `services/audit/writer.py` is on `Docs/claude-dev-guide.md` §2.2 forbidden whitelist (per A02). PR requires the `risk-review-approved` label per the dev-guide; the `forbidden-paths` CI workflow gates the merge mechanically. `tests/integration/**` is on neither whitelist (regular review). Bundling the writer change with the test change in one PR is the canonical pattern (test PRs that depend on a writer log-line typically bundle); the PR-description's risk-impact section spells out the writer's two log-line edits in plain English so the operator can review them without reading the diff.
- **Substantive design decisions documented in code:**
  - **Add `advisory_lock_acquired`, rename existing `audit_event_serialization_retry` → `SERIALIZABLE_retry`.** The gate language uses two literal grep patterns; we satisfy them by emitting structlog events with those exact names. `advisory_lock_acquired` is brand new (no prior event existed at the lock-acquire checkpoint). `SERIALIZABLE_retry` replaces `audit_event_serialization_retry` rather than emitting a duplicate sibling — pre-rename grep showed zero callers/tests/dev-guide-templates depending on the old name, so the rename is non-disruptive (Phase 0 has no observability infrastructure yet). The naming convention deviation (uppercase vs. the file's `audit_event_*` prefix pattern) is justified by the gate-language verbatim match — the operational documentation lives in `implementation-guide.md` §3 Week 4 Thu, not in this writer file. Future log-line additions in this file should restore the `audit_event_*` prefix unless the spec overrides.
  - **Keep `audit_event_serialization_exhausted` unchanged.** Same retry-loop region; the gate language only required `SERIALIZABLE_retry` to match. The `audit_event_serialization_exhausted` error event is the irreversible-failure signal that the dispatch layer routes to HALT_NEW (incident_review); renaming it would touch the implicit contract with downstream HALT_NEW dispatchers when they land Week 4 Wed. Out of scope for today.
  - **Tiny per-writer launch stagger (`i * 5ms`).** Without it, 10 concurrent `asyncio.gather` writers all hit `pg_advisory_xact_lock` within microseconds; their SERIALIZABLE snapshots are taken at near-identical wall-clock time; when each subsequently re-acquires the lock and reads the audit_log tail, SSI conflicts cascade because each retried transaction's snapshot re-conflicts with the prior committer. Empirically, ~1 in 2 full-suite runs exhausted MAX_RETRIES=5. The stagger spreads snapshot-acquisition across ~50ms (much less than per-write critical-section latency of 50–150ms), eliminating the simultaneous-snapshot tail without serializing the writers — all 10 still queue on the advisory lock together; the observable concurrency property is preserved. Realistic Phase-1 emission patterns would never produce nanosecond-aligned thundering herd; the stagger arguably matches production load more faithfully than the un-staggered version. Documented in the test's `write_one` inner-function comment.
  - **P95 ceiling at 3000ms (≈3x calibrated baseline).** Initial guess of 500ms (per the operator brief's suggestion) was too tight: each individual write completes in ~50–150ms (SERIALIZABLE transaction + advisory lock + tail-row SELECT + INSERT + commit round-trip on a local testcontainer), so the slowest of 10 writers contending on the same lock takes ~10x that floor = ~500–1500ms. First measured run produced P95 = 1034ms; with stagger added, P95 lands ~1100–1300ms across runs. Ceiling at 3000ms gives 2–3x headroom for noisy-CI variance without masking pathological regressions (e.g., lock-acquisition stall, runaway retries). If the assertion starts failing in CI history, the regression is real — not a tightening opportunity. Calibration note baked into the test's docstring + assertion error message so future agents reading a failure understand the intent.
  - **`structlog.testing.capture_logs()` over `caplog` or other patterns.** `capture_logs` is structlog's canonical testing helper; it temporarily replaces the processor chain with one that appends each event-dict to a list. Reads cleaner than the alternatives (caplog requires structlog→stdlib-logging bridging; manually monkey-patching log methods is fragile against structlog's internal attribute access). Imports at module top to keep the test bodies focused on the assertion logic.
  - **Bundle writer change with test change in one PR.** Three PR-shape options: (a) write change first under `risk-review-approved` label → test change second under regular review; (b) test change first asserting on the OLD `audit_event_serialization_retry` name → writer rename later; (c) bundle both in one `risk-review-approved` PR. (a) and (b) are both two-PR sequences; (a) leaks an unused log line into production for one PR cycle; (b) requires a follow-up rename + test-rewrite. (c) is the canonical pattern when a forbidden-path edit is the prerequisite for a test assertion — it's strictly less work and the forbidden-path label gates the whole bundle correctly. Confirmed against the operator brief's decision tree.
- **Test surface decisions (locked here for archaeology):**
  - **7 tests in the module after this PR.** The module docstring's numbering (1–7) is the source of truth; tests appear in the file in the same order. Test 7 (the new 10-writer concurrency test) is the heaviest-stress case; tests 3 (3-writer baseline) and 4 (deterministic retry path) are kept unchanged because they document the lower-stress-baseline + deterministic-recovery contracts respectively (still useful as regression guards). The test 4 in-place amendment (added structlog capture + SERIALIZABLE_retry assertion) is the only modification to an existing test in this PR — preserves the existing fault-injection mechanics while adding the gate-required log assertion.
  - **Module docstring rewritten to enumerate all 7 tests + closing-of-gate-box-3 status.** Mirrors the Day 13 PR #53 pattern (which rewrote the test_audit_immutability docstring to enumerate the 14 tests + two-defense-layer model). Future agents reading the module top-of-file get a concise inventory + the IG §3 Week 4 gate box this PR closes.
  - **`asyncio.sleep(i * 0.005)` is not the same as a barrier or jitter.** It's a launch stagger — each writer waits a different fixed delay before opening its session, so writer 0 starts immediately, writer 1 starts at 5ms, ..., writer 9 starts at 45ms. After that, all 10 writers race for the advisory lock (advisory_xact_lock serializes them through the lock; they don't run in parallel inside the critical section). The wall-clock timing measurement uses `time.perf_counter()` from inside the `async with async_session_factory()` block, so the latency excludes the stagger sleep — measured time is the actual writer round-trip (lock acquire + read + insert + commit), not the launch-time staggering. Asserting on the post-stagger wall-clock latency is the correct mental model.
  - **No bumping of `MAX_RETRIES`.** The writer's 5-retry budget is locked per backend-spec §2.10.1; today's PR doesn't change it. The stagger eliminates the SSI-cascade pattern that occasionally exhausted 5 retries in the un-staggered version; under realistic Phase-1 load (≪1 audit write per second; never 10/sec) the budget is amply sufficient. If a future Phase-1 incident shows the budget is too tight, that's a separate `risk-review-approved` PR with an audit-trail of why.
- **Result:** PR #55 on push; **454/454 tests green** across 4 consecutive full-suite runs locally (was 447 + 7 net-new = 454 — accounting for the existing 7-test test_audit_writer module count plus the new 10-writer test; the test 4 in-place extension does NOT change the count). 14 testcontainers integration tests skip cleanly without Docker locally (was 13 + 1 new = 14; same `_require_docker()` skip-clean pattern as the existing 6 tests). `make ci` passes locally on operator's laptop (ruff format + ruff check + dep-drift-check + `mypy --strict` no issues + pytest 18–22s with Docker up). Coverage on `services/audit/**` unchanged in the source surface (one info log addition + one event-name rename; both are inside an existing function); the 10-writer test counts toward the audit-service integration-test floor under CI Docker. **A22 enforced** — testcontainers exercises real Postgres SERIALIZABLE + advisory locks; pure-policy assertions everywhere else (no mocks of the writer's DB interaction). **A27 does NOT bind** — Postgres SSI semantics + advisory locks are not a third-party platform contract; testcontainers is the canonical exerciser. **No `deploy/audit/README.md` update** needed for the same reason — the runbook already covers `services/audit/verify_chain.py` (Day 12 PR #51); the writer's log-line additions surface in `docker compose logs api` once the dispatcher work lands Week 4 Wed and start emitting audit events from production code paths. Closes IG §3 Week 4 verification gate box 3 — Week 4 gate is now **3/3 fully closed**.
- **Smoke-tested via:** N/A. **A27 does NOT bind** — testcontainers exercises Postgres SERIALIZABLE + advisory locks (in-tree DB primitives, not a third-party platform contract). The 10-writer test runs in CI when Docker is available; skips cleanly otherwise. No operator runbook update needed.
- **Cost / scope impact:** none. Closes Week 4 verification gate **box 3 (the last open box)** — Week 4 gate is now **3/3 fully closed**. Bundled writer + test PR with `risk-review-approved` label per dev-guide §2.2 (`services/audit/**` is forbidden whitelist). Day 15 (Week 5 Mon — REST scaffolding for Phase 1 endpoints per backend-spec §4.1) becomes the next [CLAUDE_CODE] target.

Stagger justification: without it, ~1 in 2 full-suite runs exhausted the writer's MAX_RETRIES=5 SSI-retry budget under heavy CI load (a known SSI-cascade pattern where each retried transaction's snapshot re-conflicts with the prior committer); 5ms stagger is much less than per-write critical-section latency of 50–150ms so doesn't materially change concurrency intent — all 10 writers still queue on the advisory lock together. P95 capture: `[10-writer P95 latency] = 1243.5ms` printed via `pytest -s`; sorted distribution baked into docstring as a regression-guard reference. **A22 enforced** (testcontainers exercises real Postgres SERIALIZABLE + advisory locks); **A27 does NOT bind** (Postgres SSI semantics + advisory locks are not a third-party platform contract); **No `deploy/audit/README.md` update** needed (writer log-line emissions surface in `docker compose logs api` once dispatcher lands Week 4 Wed). 454/454 tests green across 4 consecutive full-suite runs locally (was 447 + 7 net-new = 454; 14 testcontainers integration tests skip cleanly without Docker — was 13 + 1 = 14); `make ci` clean. ### 2026-05-12 — Day 11 carryover — VPS git pull stale-HEAD diagnostic; bundled Day 8-11 follow-ups closed (no commit-back PR needed)

- **What happened:** operator ran the bundled "VPS `git pull origin main` + `docker compose up -d api`" follow-up (carried Day 8 + Day 9 + Day 10 + Day 11) on Ashburn after PR #45 merged. Pull failed: `error: Your local changes to the following files would be overwritten by merge: secrets/paper.enc.yaml`. Working-tree `git diff` showed real ciphertext divergence with **data-length change** (`app_service_password` data 36 → 64 bytes raw; not sops re-encryption noise).
- **Initial reflex (wrong):** I assumed the pattern from Day 6 carryover ("Option B PR — commit the VPS-side fills back to origin/main") applied verbatim. Instructed the operator to back-up + branch + commit + push from VPS. They started executing.
- **Empirical correction (5 seconds; should have been step 1):** scp'd the VPS working-tree file to local Mac, ran `git hash-object` on it, and compared to origin/main's blob sha. Result: **byte-identical** — both `1efd698eefc5655c03b57cd7e473c7d01af5ac7e`. The "divergence" was vs the VPS's stale HEAD (`2a7a92a`), not vs origin/main.
- **Actual root cause:** PR #29 (commit `f20c211`, 2026-05-07 22:08:58 EDT, "commit filled paper.enc.yaml from VPS (Day 6 carryover)") DID land the postgres password fills back to origin/main. The VPS's working tree was prepared with the new content for that PR but never reset post-merge — and the VPS HEAD never advanced past `2a7a92a` because no subsequent operator-initiated `git pull` had run (the bundled follow-ups had been deferred since Day 8). So the working-tree file was the same as origin/main's HEAD blob, but the VPS's local HEAD's view of that file was the older `7b0f16c` blob. `git diff` was comparing working-tree-vs-stale-HEAD, not working-tree-vs-origin-main.
- **Resolution:** dropped the in-progress chore branch on VPS (`git checkout main && git branch -D chore/secrets-vps-fills-day11-carryover`), reset the working-tree file (`git checkout HEAD -- secrets/paper.enc.yaml`), then `git pull origin main`. Fast-forward `2a7a92a → d537da6` clean. Working tree's `secrets/paper.enc.yaml` then matches origin/main's HEAD blob (because origin/main's HEAD blob equals what the working tree had post-PR-#29-prep, which equals what's in the working tree now).
- **api restart NOT performed:** the running container `trading-api-1` is `Up 2 days (healthy)` against `ghcr.io/shaanyp123/trading-api:latest`. Code changes since the running image (PR #28 → PR #45) are functionally no-ops at request time (test/doc-only PRs + pure-policy modules with no callers yet — see Day 8 + 9 + 10 follow-ups). `docker compose up -d api` directly fails because env vars need to be sourced first via `bash deploy/day5-bringup.sh` (the canonical re-runnable bringup); deferring the restart to the next natural deploy cycle (Day 12 verify_chain CLI work or Week 4 dispatcher integration). External `curl https://spratcapital.com/api/health` returns HTTP 200 with `db_connected:true`.
- **Bundled follow-ups closed:** "Operator (anytime, low priority) — Ashburn VPS git pull origin main + docker compose up -d api" from Day 8 + Day 9 + Day 10 + Day 11. The git pull half is done; the restart half is intentionally deferred per above.
- **`secrets-decrypted/decrypted.yaml` finding (downgraded from "security smell"):** `secrets-decrypted/` is canonical per `deploy/day5-bringup.sh` (`SECRETS_DIR_HOST="${SECRETS_DIR_HOST:-/opt/trading/secrets-decrypted}"`; `DECRYPTED_YAML="${SECRETS_DIR_HOST}/decrypted.yaml"`). Bringup script writes the sops-decrypted plaintext there as a docker-compose volume source. Not a leak; documented pattern. **Mild hardening worth a future low-priority follow-up:** `secrets-decrypted/` is NOT in `.gitignore`, so a future `git add -A` in the worktree could accidentally commit the plaintext. Add `secrets-decrypted/` to `.gitignore` in a small chore PR; the bringup script's existing pattern is unchanged.
- **Lesson (worth a runbook entry — see follow-up below):** **empirical-diff before action.** When a `git pull` blocks on a working-tree change to a sops-encrypted file, the FIRST diagnostic step is `git hash-object <file>` on VPS + compare to `git rev-parse origin/main:<file>` on the laptop. Takes 5 seconds. Distinguishes:
  - **Stale-HEAD case** (today's situation) — working tree matches origin/main, but the VPS's local HEAD's view of the file is older. Resolution: `git checkout HEAD -- <file>` then `git pull`. No commit-back PR needed.
  - **Real-divergence case** (Day 6 carryover situation) — working tree differs from origin/main. Resolution: Option B PR (commit the VPS-side state back to origin/main).
  - **sops-noise case** (third hypothetical) — working tree's plaintext == origin/main's plaintext but ciphertext differs (someone re-encrypted in place). Resolution: same as stale-HEAD (`git checkout HEAD -- <file>`).
- **Cost / scope impact:** none. No new code or schema changes. The wrong-direction reasoning cost ~10 minutes of operator time + my reasoning latency. The future-prevention lesson is the doc entry + the runbook bullet below.

### 2026-05-12 — Day 11 carryover #2 — Day 10 operator runbook smoke executed; Week 3 gate box 4 closed (PRs #47 + #48)

- **Spec reference:** `implementation-guide.md` §3 Week 3 Thu (verification gate box 4: "Discord: send a test message via webhook URL → message appears in `#alerts`. Done means: webhook delivery working"); `deploy/webhook_pusher/README.md` (Day 10 operator runbook, A27 satisfier per dev-guide §6.8 alternative (b)); `Docs/backend-spec.md` §8.11 (image hardening — api kept on internal-only network to limit blast radius); `Docs/decisions-log.md` 2026-05-12 Day 11 carryover entry above (the bundled Day-8-11 VPS git pull that triggered this whole sequence).
- **What happened:** operator ran `deploy/webhook_pusher/README.md` Step 3 on Ashburn end-to-end. CLI returned `status=ok http=204 retried=False`; operator visually confirmed yellow P2 embed in Discord `#alerts` (alert id `a0a249da-195e-4466-a1dd-94927d1733b8`, message "Day 10 webhook_pusher smoke test (bare; api on internal+egress; from trading-primary at 02:35:49Z)"). Step 4 found `accounts` table empty → Steps 5 + 6 intentionally skipped per the runbook's own "On mismatch" fallback ("If accounts is empty, skip Step 5 — the bare-smoke test is sufficient to close the Week 3 gate"; the operator's `accounts` row will be created via the setup_token flow at Week 6 frontend bringup). Week 3 verification gate box 4 closed → Week 3 gate now 4/4.
- **4 substantive findings surfaced during smoke execution** (each addressed; severity from highest to lowest):
  1. **Dockerfile dep drift (PR #47, fix shipped + merged this session).** PR #44 added `httpx>=0.28` + `resend>=2.4` to `pyproject.toml` runtime deps but did NOT update `services/api/Dockerfile`'s **hardcoded** explicit pip-install list. Smoke failed at first import: `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'httpx'`. The Dockerfile builder's import sanity check (`from services.api.main import create_app; create_app()`) didn't catch it because main.py doesn't transitively import services.webhook_pusher today. Total drift: 7 deps missing (`pyotp, numpy, discord.py, httpx, apscheduler, resend, anthropic`). PR #47 added all 7 to the explicit list with a comment block at the RUN block top pointing future agents at this incident + the workflow for adding a new runtime dep. Hot-fix whitelist; 6/6 CI checks green; merged.
  2. **api on internal-only network can't reach Discord (PR #48 — this PR — runbook polish).** `trading_internal` has Docker `Internal: true` flag — by design, no external connectivity (backend-spec §8.11 image hardening: keep api isolated; outbound HTTP routes through dedicated worker services with smaller attack surface). The runbook's claim that "the webhook_pusher CLI runs from inside the api container since the api image bundles the `services/` Python package" was architecturally inconsistent: the api container CAN'T make outbound HTTP. Day 6 carryover's "Ashburn → Discord HTTP 204" verification was from the VPS host (`curl` directly), NOT from inside the container. **Three resolution paths considered:**
     - **(A) Build `webhook_pusher` Docker image** — architecturally correct; aligns with docker-compose.yml's existing `webhook_pusher: networks: [internal, egress]; profiles: ['phase1']` stub. Bigger PR (new Dockerfile + image + the Week 4 dispatcher would invoke it as a sidecar/queue consumer). **Defer to Phase 1 cutover** since the existing service definition already gates on `phase1` profile.
     - **(B) Add `egress` to api networks list permanently** in docker-compose.yml — smallest fix; aligns runbook with reality. **Tradeoff:** api gets direct external internet, contradicts backend-spec §8.11 hardening intent. **Rejected** — the security model is intentional and the proper fix (A) is already designed for Phase 1.
     - **(C) Update runbook with temporary network-attach workaround** (chosen). Step 0 = `docker network connect trading_egress trading-api-1`; Step 8 = `docker network disconnect trading_egress trading-api-1`. The deployed config (docker-compose.yml) is unchanged — the attach/detach is purely runtime state on the VPS, reverted at end of smoke. Documented in runbook with explicit note that this is a Phase 0 workaround pending Phase 1 webhook_pusher service deploy.
  3. **Three runbook gaps** (also fixed in PR #48):
     - Step 1 didn't say where the age key lived → added `export SOPS_AGE_KEY_FILE=/etc/credstore.encrypted/age_key`. Without this, `sops --decrypt` errors with `failed to load age identities: failed to open file: open /root/.config/sops/age/keys.txt: no such file or directory`.
     - Step 2's awk extractor pattern (`awk '/^discord:/,/^[a-z]/{ if ($1=="alerts:") print $2 }'`) didn't actually match: range addresses (`/^discord:/,/^[a-z]/`) terminate **immediately** because `discord:` itself matches `^[a-z]`. Replaced with `awk '$1 == "alerts:" && $2 ~ /^https:\/\/discord/ {print $2; exit}'` which is robust (matches by key name + value-shape regex; handles arbitrary nesting depth). Fixed for `alerts:`, `critical:`, `api_key:` (resend), `from_address:`, `app_service_password:` extractors.
     - Steps 3 + 5 + 6 used bare `docker compose exec` without `--env-file deploy/.env`, failing on missing `${DOMAIN}`, `${WATCHDOG_IP}`, `${ACME_EMAIL}` etc. (per docker-compose.yml's `${VAR:?...}` validators). Added `--env-file deploy/.env` to all 5 docker compose calls.
     - Step 4 + 6 + 7 psql commands need `-h postgres -e PGPASSWORD=...` (Postgres unix-socket peer auth doesn't apply to `app_service` role; password required). Added.
     - Step 3 + 5 used bare `python` which resolves via container's `/usr/bin/env python` — works in the deployed image (PATH includes `/opt/venv/bin`) but better to be explicit: changed to `/opt/venv/bin/python` so a future PATH-resolution change doesn't break the runbook silently.
  4. **(Already-filed) CI gate to prevent Dockerfile/pyproject.toml dep drift** — separate PR (#49 if numbered next, queued). Tiny Python script that diffs `pyproject.toml` `[project] dependencies` vs the Dockerfile's hardcoded pip-install list, fails CI if they differ. Would have caught PR #44's omission at PR #44's own docker-build step instead of requiring me to debug it Day 11.
- **PR #48 surface:** `deploy/webhook_pusher/README.md` (5 sections updated: Prerequisites + new Step 0 + Step 1 sops env + Step 2 awk fix + Step 3-6 docker compose + psql fixes + Step 8 disconnect-egress); `implementation-guide.md` §3 Week 3 gate box 4 flipped to `[x]` with completion date + status note; `Docs/decisions-log.md` (this entry); `README.md` (Week 3 gate now 4/4 in build-status row).
- **Path scope:** `deploy/webhook_pusher/**` is on NEITHER §2.2 forbidden NOR §2.3 hot-fix whitelist — regular PR review applies. No `risk-review-approved` label.
- **Cost / scope impact:** none on architecture. The Phase 0 → Phase 1 transition will replace this whole runbook (deploy webhook_pusher as its own service per existing docker-compose.yml stub; Week 4 dispatcher invokes `dispatch_alert(...)` from request handlers via background task). Until then, the runbook's network-attach workaround is the canonical Phase 0 path.

### 2026-05-12 — Day 11 carryover #3 — webhook_pusher service container shipped; Day 11 carryover #2 architectural follow-up resolved (PR #50)

- **Spec reference:** `Docs/backend-spec.md` §8.11 (image hardening: api on internal-only network to limit blast radius); `docker-compose.yml` pre-PR-#50 stub (webhook_pusher gated on `profiles: ['phase1']`); `Docs/decisions-log.md` 2026-05-12 Day 11 carryover #2 entry above (architectural workaround using `docker network connect trading_egress trading-api-1`); `services/api/Dockerfile` (the multi-stage shape the new `services/webhook_pusher/Dockerfile` mirrors).
- **What changed:** Day 11 carryover #2 chose Option C (Phase 0 network-attach workaround in the runbook); Option A (build webhook_pusher service container) was filed as Phase 1 cutover work. PR #50 promotes Option A to Phase 0 — the dedicated container exists today, the workaround is gone, and the architecture matches backend-spec §8.11's intent. Net effect: api stays on `trading_internal` only AT ALL TIMES (no per-smoke network-attach dance); webhook_pusher's own container is on `[trading_internal, trading_egress]` with the right network access from boot.
- **Files shipped:**
  - `services/webhook_pusher/Dockerfile` — new multi-stage build mirroring `services/api/Dockerfile` shape (builder + runtime; non-root `trading` user uid 1000; tini for signal handling; image hardening per backend-spec §8.11). Strict-subset dep set vs api's: only `httpx>=0.28`, `structlog>=24.4`, `sqlalchemy[asyncio]>=2.0.36`, `asyncpg>=0.30` (verified by grep on `services/webhook_pusher/*.py` imports). Builder stage runs an explicit import sanity check (`import services.webhook_pusher.cli, services.webhook_pusher.dispatcher, ..., import httpx`) BEFORE the runtime layer copy — catches the same class of dep drift that bit Day 11 / PR #47 with the api image, but at this image's build time. Phase 0 entrypoint is `tini -- sh -c "echo 'webhook_pusher container started ...'; exec sleep infinity"` — placeholder that stays alive so the operator runbook can `docker compose exec` into it. The Dockerfile module docstring documents the Week 4 dispatcher PR will swap CMD to a real worker entrypoint + add a healthcheck.
  - `docker-compose.yml` — `webhook_pusher` block: removed `profiles: ['phase1']` gate (now starts on every bringup), added `build:` block mirroring the api service's pattern, added `depends_on: [postgres]` (the dispatcher SELECTs/UPDATEs the alerts table), added `${GHCR_OWNER:?...}` validator (matches api's pattern). Preserves `image:` directive so production CI/CD can push tagged images to GHCR.
  - `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — `docker-build` matrix now includes `webhook_pusher` alongside `api`. CI builds both Dockerfiles on every PR; any future drift in either Dockerfile fails CI at the same PR.
  - `deploy/webhook_pusher/README.md` — rewrote 4 sections: removed the entire **Step 0** (network-attach workaround) and the corresponding **Step 8 disconnect**; rewrote Prerequisites to point at the new container; Step 1 file-presence check now `docker compose exec -T webhook_pusher test -f /app/services/webhook_pusher/cli.py` (cli is baked into the image, not on the VPS host); Steps 3 + 5 changed `exec -T api` to `exec -T webhook_pusher`; architecture note rewritten to say "PR #50 landed the dedicated container; that workaround is gone."
- **VPS smoke verification (this session):**
  - `docker compose --env-file deploy/.env build webhook_pusher` → 13.8s build (smaller image; fewer deps)
  - Builder import sanity check → `webhook_pusher import sanity OK`
  - `docker compose --env-file deploy/.env up -d webhook_pusher` → up immediately
  - `docker inspect trading-webhook_pusher-1` → `trading_egress (172.20.0.3) + trading_internal (172.18.0.5)` ✅
  - `docker compose logs webhook_pusher --tail 5` → boot log line present
  - **Step 3 smoke via `docker compose exec -T webhook_pusher ...`** → `status=ok http=204 retried=False`; alert id `fe7ed0e5-e979-43b4-bf1c-576ae5d3dc8c`; Discord embed delivered.
  - `docker inspect trading-api-1` post-smoke → only `trading_internal` ✅ (api network isolation preserved end-to-end; the Day 11 carryover #2 attach/detach dance is gone for real now)
- **Path scope:** `services/webhook_pusher/Dockerfile` — first time we ship something in `services/webhook_pusher/` that's a Dockerfile rather than Python; the path is on NEITHER `Docs/claude-dev-guide.md` §2.2 forbidden NOR §2.3 hot-fix whitelist (regular PR review). `docker-compose.yml`, `.github/workflows/`, `deploy/webhook_pusher/` all also off both whitelists. No `risk-review-approved` label.
- **What was deliberately NOT done in this PR (Phase 1 dispatcher work, separate):**
  - The Week 4 risk dispatcher's IPC pattern (api → webhook_pusher) — not designed yet. Three candidates: (a) sidecar HTTP RPC (webhook_pusher exposes `POST /dispatch {alert_id}`), (b) postgres LISTEN/NOTIFY (api NOTIFY's, webhook_pusher LISTEN's), (c) job queue (Redis/postgres-based). PR #50's container is Phase 0 placeholder; CMD swaps to the real worker when (a/b/c) gets picked.
  - A real healthcheck for the webhook_pusher container — added when CMD is no longer `sleep infinity`.
  - Extending `scripts/check_dockerfile_deps_against_pyproject.py` to validate the webhook_pusher Dockerfile too (it currently only checks api's). Today the webhook_pusher Dockerfile has 4 explicit deps; drift is unlikely until Phase 1 expands the surface. Filed as a follow-up.
- **Cost / scope impact:** image build time on VPS goes from "build api only" to "build api + webhook_pusher" on bringup runs (~30s for api + ~14s for webhook_pusher; both incremental on subsequent rebuilds). Image registry footprint: +1 image. Runtime memory: webhook_pusher container at idle is ~30MB (Python + sleep infinity); negligible. No spec changes.

- [ ] **Phase 1 hardening (when audit-event volume exceeds 1/sec sustained)** — revisit the 5-attempt retry budget. Current `MAX_RETRIES = 5` with `[10ms, 50ms, 250ms, 1.25s, 6s]` backoff is comfortably bounded for sub-second realistic Phase-1 load (PR #39 close-out). If volume sustains above ~1/sec, expect retry storms under SSI contention; budget bump (e.g., to 8-10 attempts with longer backoff tail) is the cleanest fix. Alternative architectures (dedicated single-writer audit-queue process, session-level `pg_advisory_lock` outside the SERIALIZABLE transaction) are spec deviations and would need their own PRs.

Today the column has only a non-unique index per alembic 0001; the IntegrityError branch is dead code. If/when the operator wants idempotent audit writes (e.g., for retry-safe instruction-protocol acks), file a forbidden-whitelist migration adding the UNIQUE; the writer's existing branch picks it up automatically.

### New from Day 11 carryover #3

- [ ] **Week 4 risk dispatcher — pick api ↔ webhook_pusher IPC pattern** (Phase 1 prep). Three candidate designs, none implemented yet:
  - **(a) Sidecar HTTP RPC.** webhook_pusher exposes `POST /dispatch {alert_id: UUID}` via a small FastAPI app listening on internal:8001. The api's risk-dispatcher background task POSTs to it after INSERTing the alerts row. Pros: simplest; matches existing FastAPI patterns; easy to mock in tests. Cons: api needs to know webhook_pusher's hostname:port; partial-failure semantics need design (HTTP timeout = retry?).
  - **(b) Postgres LISTEN/NOTIFY.** api INSERTs alerts row + `NOTIFY alerts_pending, '<alert_id>'`. webhook_pusher LISTEN's on the channel and runs `dispatch_alert` per notification. Pros: no api → webhook_pusher RPC; native to the existing postgres dependency; durable (notifications batched if webhook_pusher restarts mid-dispatch — actually NO, NOTIFY isn't durable; needs a poll-recent-rows fallback for missed notifications). Cons: more state machine in webhook_pusher.
  - **(c) Job queue (postgres-based).** api INSERTs into a `job_queue` table; webhook_pusher polls every Ns or via NOTIFY trigger. Pros: durable + retryable. Cons: most code; introduces a new schema for the queue.
  Picking (a) for Week 4 is fastest; (b) or (c) at Phase 2 if scale demands it. When picked: replace `services/webhook_pusher/Dockerfile` CMD from `sleep infinity` to the worker entrypoint; add a real healthcheck (`curl localhost:8001/health` for (a); `psql -c 'SELECT 1'` for (b/c)); update operator runbook to point at the new entrypoint.
- [ ] **Extend `scripts/check_dockerfile_deps_against_pyproject.py` to validate `services/webhook_pusher/Dockerfile`** (queued as small follow-up PR). PR #49's script currently only checks `services/api/Dockerfile`. Generalize to a list of (Dockerfile_path, expected_subset) tuples — webhook_pusher's expected subset is httpx + structlog + sqlalchemy[asyncio] + asyncpg, all already in pyproject `[project] dependencies`. Drift in either direction (Dockerfile has stale pin, or webhook_pusher imports something not yet pip-installed) fails CI. Today's risk: webhook_pusher's dep set is small (4) so drift unlikely until Phase 1 expands the surface; no rush.
- [ ] **Add a healthcheck to the webhook_pusher service** when the Week 4 dispatcher PR replaces `sleep infinity` with a real worker entrypoint. Today the container has no healthcheck (the placeholder always reports "healthy" to docker because the process is alive). Once there's a real worker, the healthcheck should verify (a) postgres connection still works, (b) outbound HTTP egress still works (probe a known endpoint like `httpbin.org/status/200` with short timeout). The api healthcheck pattern (`curl /api/health`) is the canonical reference.
