### 2026-05-05 — Day 3 close-out — branch protection required-checks gap closed

- **Spec reference:** `implementation-guide.md` §11 Day 1 ("require CI to pass; no direct push to `main`"); 2026-05-05 Day 1 decisions-log entry "Branch protection rules applied to `main`".
- **Pre-existing gap (discovered Day 3 close-out):** Day 1's branch protection setup added `lint (ruff)` + `gitleaks (secret scan)` to the required-status-checks list. Day 2 added two more CI jobs (`typecheck (mypy --strict)` + `test (pytest --cov)`) to `.github/workflows/ci.yml` but never updated branch protection. Result: those jobs were running on every PR but were NOT actually blocking merge — a PR with a typecheck failure or a test regression could have squeezed through if the operator merged before reading the check status. Visible only when inspecting protection state via `gh api .../branches/main/protection`.
- **Decision (this entry):** updated branch protection's required-status-checks via `gh api repos/.../branches/main/protection/required_status_checks/contexts -X POST` to include all five current CI jobs.
- **Final required-status-checks list:**
  1. `lint (ruff)` (Day 1)
  2. `gitleaks (secret scan)` (Day 1)
  3. `typecheck (mypy --strict)` (Day 2 — gap fix Day 3)
  4. `test (pytest --cov)` (Day 2 — gap fix Day 3)
  5. `forbidden-paths (risk-review-approved gate)` (Day 3, just shipped via PR #10)
- **Rationale:** every CI job that runs SHOULD also block. If we trust a check enough to run it on every PR, we trust it enough to gate merge on it. The gap was a Day-2-PR-author oversight, not a deliberate "advisory check" choice.
- **Lesson for future sessions:** every PR that adds a new CI job MUST also add the job's check name to required-status-checks in the same PR. Add as a one-line operator-action item to the PR template and to the dev-guide §1.2 commit-discipline checklist.
- **Cost / scope impact:** none.

---

## How specs reference this log

When a spec claim is now wrong or incomplete because of an entry above, the spec gets a one-line annotation pointing here. We do NOT rewrite specs to match reality — specs remain the canonical "what we agreed to build"; this log is the canonical "what we actually did."

Cross-references in current edits:

**Day 1:**
- `Docs/backend-spec.md` §1.6 → "Falkenstein (locked)" annotated with pointer to the Nuremberg deviation entry above.
- `implementation-guide.md` §2.1, §2.3 → cost table updated in-place to reflect actual prices; original spec ranges retained in this log for archaeology.

**Day 2:**
- `Docs/backend-spec.md` §2.3 (Hurst exponent) → R/S estimator (not DFA) is the implementation choice; `HURST_THRESHOLD` raised from 0.50 to 0.55 to compensate for R/S small-sample bias.
- `Docs/backend-spec.md` §2.3 + §2.4.1 (sub-universe) → V1 candidate universe LOCKED to /MES, /MNQ, /MYM, /M2K, /MGC, /MCL, /MBT + TLT, IEF, SHY, TIP. Active set is dynamic per equity tier via Stage 0 sizing.

**Day 3:**
- `Docs/backend-spec.md` §3.2 (audit_log) → `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX ... ON audit_log(sequence_no)` is invalid SQL on a partitioned table; replaced with non-unique index in migration 0001. BIGSERIAL still guarantees global uniqueness.
- `Docs/backend-spec.md` §2.10.2 (TRUNCATE block) → spec's `EVENT TRIGGER ... ON ddl_command_start WHEN TAG IN ('TRUNCATE TABLE')` is unsupported by Postgres; replaced with statement-level `BEFORE TRUNCATE` triggers attached to parent + each yearly partition in migration 0005.
- `Docs/backend-spec.md` §3.26 (universe_state) → `CHECK (... IN (..., NULL))` is invalid SQL semantically; rewritten as `IS NULL OR IN (...)` in migration 0004.
- `Docs/claude-dev-guide.md` §7.1 → updated to document hybrid migration filename convention (numeric `NNNN_` for foundational set 0001-0006, date-based `YYYY-MM-DD_` for everything Day 4+).
- `Docs/claude-dev-guide.md` §10.1 Week 3 → annotated to describe the `BEFORE TRUNCATE` trigger mechanism (replaces the spec's `EVENT TRIGGER` wording).
- `Docs/claude-dev-guide.md` §11 [A02] → previously asserted "Pre-merge linter is mechanical and will block the merge"; now actually true after `.github/workflows/forbidden-paths.yml` shipped in PR #10 + `risk-review-approved` label created Day 3.

**Day 4:**
- `implementation-guide.md` §11 Day 4 09:00 ("use QC's paper broker") → resolved to two distinct decisions (live broker = QC Paper at deploy; brokerage model = IBKR/Margin in code). Documented in `lean/README.md` operator runbook + this log.
- `Docs/backend-spec.md` §1.6 (alert behavior) → spec describes "alert at 3 consecutive failures" but does not specify repeat-alert behavior on tick 4–N during a sustained outage. Watchdog implementation adds a 60-min cooldown to suppress duplicate alerts; operator can manually reset state to force the next failure to alert immediately.
- `Docs/backend-spec.md` §1.6 + §4.5.3 (watchdog endpoints) → Day 4 watchdog uses GET `/api/health` only. POST `/api/internal/watchdog` (push) is wired in spec but not implemented Day 4 since the endpoint doesn't exist on the backend yet. Bearer token captured Day 3 (PR #11), held for Week 5+ when the endpoint lands.
- `Docs/claude-dev-guide.md` §3.5 (`structlog` mandate) → watchdog explicitly opts out (stdlib `logging` with JSON formatter). Documented as an exception in this log; rule remains in effect for backend service code.
- `lean/v1_qc_algorithm.py` (PascalCase QC API) → QC migrated its Python API to snake_case; algorithm rewritten in PR #17 with method names, enum values, and framework callbacks all snake_case. Class names remain PascalCase. Module docstring + `lean/README.md` troubleshooting table updated.
- `lean/README.md` Step 2 (filename rename) → QC Cloud's runtime loader requires the algorithm file to be named `main.py` specifically; renaming to `v1_qc_algorithm.py` for repo-filename consistency caused runtime failure. PR #18 reverted the rename instruction; the repo file keeps its descriptive name, the QC project file stays `main.py`.
- `lean/v1_qc_algorithm.py` (`time_rules.at` timezone arg) → QC's API does not accept a timezone string as a third positional argument. PR #19 dropped the redundant arg; scheduled actions inherit the algorithm's time zone from `set_time_zone()`.
- `Docs/backend-spec.md` §1.6 + `claude-dev-guide.md` §1.5 (Resend deferral) → reversed 2026-05-07 (PR #22). Discord webhooks from the Hetzner Nuremberg VPS are blocked at the Cloudflare WAF. Resend is now Phase 0's primary alert channel for the watchdog; Discord stays as best-effort secondary. See "Discord webhook POSTs blocked from Hetzner VPS by Cloudflare WAF" entry above. Backend-side Discord webhook viability (Ashburn IP) **resolved 2026-05-08**: Ashburn passes the same probe Nuremberg fails, so the backend's 6 planned Discord channels stay on Discord; only the watchdog migrated to Resend. The block is per-DC IP-reputation scoring at Cloudflare, not a Hetzner-wide rule.
- `watchdog/watchdog.py` (User-Agent on outbound POSTs) → stdlib `urllib.request` defaults to `Python-urllib/3.x` which Discord's anti-bot layer blocks with 403. PR #21 added an explicit `WATCHDOG_USER_AGENT` constant and applied it in `_post_json` so all outbound POSTs (Discord, Resend, future `/api/internal/watchdog` push) identify as `trading-watchdog/0.1.0` rather than the stdlib default.
- `watchdog/README.md` Step 5 / Step 6 ordering → original runbook ran the manual smoke test before enabling the timer; systemd's `StateDirectory=` only creates `/var/lib/trading-watchdog/` on first service activation, so the manual run failed with `PermissionError`. PR #22 reordered: timer enable now precedes smoke test.

**Day 5:**
- `docker-compose.yml` (sops_init container) → dropped 2026-05-07 (PR #26). The pinned `getsops/sops:v3.10.2` image doesn't exist on Docker Hub; sops decryption moved to the host via `deploy/day5-bringup.sh`. `sops_init` service deleted; api volumes bind-mount `${SECRETS_DIR}` directly.
- `secrets/paper.enc.yaml` (`git reset --hard` data-loss) → 2026-05-08 PR #29 commits the filled file from operator's laptop; `secrets/paper.enc.yaml` IS now the canonical filled version. `deploy/day5-bringup.sh` Step 0.5 auto-restore from `/etc/credstore.encrypted/paper.enc.yaml.backup` shipped in PR #28 as a safety net.

**Day 6-9 (PR #28):**
- `Docs/backend-spec.md` §2.4.1 (sizing pipeline) → PSD repair runs ONCE upstream of Stage 1 (not just inside Stage 3 as the spec example trace suggests), because spec text says "every Σ used for portfolio-vol or cluster shrink runs through nearest_psd"; both Stage 1 (vol scaling) and Stage 3 (cluster) use Σ. Stage 3 trace records the boolean; orchestrator emits the audit event.
- `Docs/backend-spec.md` §2.4.3 (kill-switch state machine) → 3-state model + severity column matches `risk_state` schema (§3.14), implemented in `services/risk/state_machine.py` as pure-policy plan functions. Implementation-guide §11 Day 7 prose's "5 states" (HALT_NEW_routine / HALT_NEW_defenv / HALT_NEW_incident / NORMAL / CONVALESCENT) was prose shorthand for state+severity tuples.
- `Docs/backend-spec.md` §3.13 (decision_diary) → tag enum is the SPEC's `data_concern / regime_concern / size_concern / manual_judgment / other`. Implementation-guide §11 Day 9 prompt's alternative tags (signal_override, parameter_change_reviewed, halt_acknowledgement, etc.) would fail the alembic 0003 CHECK constraint. NO separate `decision_diary_logged` audit_log event (not in §3.30 taxonomy).
- `Docs/backend-spec.md` §3.18 (vacation_mode) → `vacation_started` / `vacation_ended` from locked taxonomy §3.30 (NOT the IG's `vacation_mode_toggled`). end-vacation policy rejects Discord path (re-auth web-only per dev-guide §1.5).
- `Docs/backend-spec.md` §2.9 + §3.28 (calendar) → schedules are 22:00 ET import + 23:00 ET cutoff (spec); IG §11 Day 9 prompt's 20:00 ET / 16:00 ET ignored. Audit event names: `calendar_imported` (NOT `calendar_event_imported`).
- `implementation-guide.md` §11 Day 6 11:00 (verify_universe.py bond list) → script imports `V1_CANDIDATE_UNIVERSE` from `parameters.py` (TLT/IEF/SHY/TIP locked 2026-05-05); IG's `/ZN /ZB /ZF /ZT` Treasury-futures list is superseded.
- `pyproject.toml` (numpy>=2.1 added) → `services/risk/sizing.py` PSD repair via `np.linalg.eigh`. Pure-Python eigendecomposition is impractical; numpy is the canonical choice. Same dep will be needed for Week 4+ slippage calibration OLS.

**Day 8 (PRs #39 + #40):**
- `Docs/claude-dev-guide.md` §5.1 (audit-log writer; catches `OperationalError`) → asyncpg's `SerializationError` is mapped to a generic `DBAPIError` by SQLAlchemy's asyncpg dialect (`PostgresError` → `dialect.Error`, NOT `OperationalError`). `services/audit/writer.py` catches `DBAPIError` and detects via `error.orig.sqlstate == "40001"`. Following the §5.1 example verbatim against asyncpg silently misses every serialization failure.
- `Docs/claude-dev-guide.md` §7.3 (`import jcs` from PyJCS) → `services/audit/chain.py` ships an in-tree JCS canonicalizer instead. PyJCS raises `TypeError` on `Decimal` (the trading system uses Decimal pervasively for money/price per A05); the in-tree implementation converts Decimal → `str()` canonical repr and rejects `float` loudly. ASCII-only payload key set means Python's natural string sort matches RFC 8785's UTF-16 code-unit ordering for BMP characters.
- `Docs/backend-spec.md` §2.10.1 (write path) → SERIALIZABLE + `pg_advisory_xact_lock` does NOT eliminate SSI conflicts under concurrent contention because the lock call is itself the snapshot-taking statement. The retry loop is mandatory, not optional. Realistic Phase-1 audit-write rate (sub-second per write) is comfortably bounded by `MAX_RETRIES = 5`; under heavier artificial concurrency the budget can exhaust. Documented in writer module docstring.
- `Docs/backend-spec.md` §3.2 (audit_log + `event_uuid` index) → spec calls for `CREATE INDEX audit_log_event_uuid_idx` (non-unique), so the writer's `IntegrityError` idempotency branch (read back existing row by UUID instead of inserting a duplicate) is currently dead code. If/when a future migration adds `UNIQUE(event_uuid)` for retry-safe instruction-protocol acks, the existing branch picks it up automatically.
- `Docs/backend-spec.md` §3.19 (qc_adapter_cursor seed) → already inserted by `0004_ops_tables.py` at table creation. PR #40's `2026-05-09_qc_adapter_cursor_seed.py` is structured as a defensive idempotent re-seed (`ON CONFLICT (directory_path) DO NOTHING` upgrade + intentional no-op downgrade) rather than a fresh seed.
- `Docs/claude-dev-guide.md` §7.1 (hybrid migration filename) → first **operational** dated migration shipped: `2026-05-09_qc_adapter_cursor_seed.py`. Numeric foundational series 0001-0006 sealed. All future migrations follow `YYYY-MM-DD_<short>.py` per the Day 3 lock.

**Day 9 (PR #42):**
- `Docs/claude-dev-guide.md` §5.5 (Reconciliation Diff reference; time-based T+1 grace via `_is_within_t1_grace(flexquery_eod)`) → `services/reconciliation/recon.py` implements **data-based** T+1 grace instead: a break matching a `PriorBreak` (same `(metric, market, delta)` tuple) is tagged `grace_period`. Operational intent matches the spec ("first detection halts; persistent breaks during recovery don't re-trigger"); the dev-guide reference's time-based `report_date + 24h` check is impractical for the pure-policy module (no time-now dependency keeps it trivially testable per A22). Test surface `tests/unit/test_reconciliation.py::TestT1Grace` documents the contract; revisit if Phase 1 ops shows edge cases (see "T+1 grace semantics revisit" follow-up above).
- `Docs/claude-dev-guide.md` §5.5 (calls `append_audit_event` and `invoke_kill_switch` directly) → `recon.py` ships pure-policy plan-then-apply instead, returning `ReconciliationPlan` with `audit_events: tuple[PendingAuditEvent, ...]` + `should_invoke_kill_switch: bool` as data. Caller (Week 4 dispatcher) owns the writer call + kill-switch invocation. Same shape as PR #28's state_machine / vacation / calendar_import / sizing and PR #37's qc_adapter/poll. Anti-pattern A22 enforced — unit tests inspect the plan struct, no audit writes from tests, no testcontainers dependency.
- `Docs/backend-spec.md` §2.6 (Reconciliation Tolerances Table — "Position qty 0 tolerance, cash $5 / 1bps abs, T+1 grace, dividend ex-date 2× widening") → recon.py module-level constants `POSITION_QTY_TOLERANCE = Decimal("0")`, `CASH_ABS_TOLERANCE_USD = Decimal("5.00")`, `CASH_BPS_TOLERANCE = Decimal("0.0001")`, `DIVIDEND_WIDENING_FACTOR = Decimal("2")`. Test class `TestLockedConstants` asserts these equal the spec values — drift would fail the test before any policy test runs.
- `Docs/backend-spec.md` §3.15 (`reconciliation_breaks.resolution_path` CHECK: `'grace_period','manual','kill_switch','tolerance_widened_dividend'`) → `ResolutionPath` StrEnum mirrors the four values verbatim. The policy module assigns `grace_period` to T+1-matching breaks; `manual` and `kill_switch` are caller/operator concerns; `tolerance_widened_dividend` is reserved for an admitted-but-tracked workflow not implemented today (the spec's intent for that value is unclear at this layer, so the policy module simply doesn't emit it — caller can override at the dispatcher boundary if needed).
- `Docs/backend-spec.md` §3.24 (`dividend_history.ex_date`) → caller seeds the `dividend_ex_date_today: bool` parameter from `SELECT 1 FROM dividend_history WHERE ex_date = current_date_nyse LIMIT 1`. Empty `dividend_history` is permissible (dividend_ex_date_today defaults False), but the recon dispatcher should populate the table for V1 ETFs (TLT/IEF/SHY/TIP) before Week 8 live cutover — see "Week 4 hygiene (when dispatcher seeds dividend_ex_date_today)" follow-up above.
- `Docs/backend-spec.md` §3.30 (audit event taxonomy) → `recon.py` emits exactly three event types: `reconciliation_check_passed`, `reconciliation_break_detected`, `reconciliation_break_resolved` — all three are values in `services.audit.event_types.AuditEventType`. Test `test_event_types_are_canonical_taxonomy` validates programmatically by constructing `AuditEventType(emitted_value)` for each emitted type (raises if not in enum).

**Day 11 (PR #45):**
- `implementation-guide.md` §3 Week 4 Mon (representative event prose: `signal_emitted`, `fill_received`, `position_reconciled`, `kill_switch_triggered`, `session_end`) → fixture filenames + test case names use the **locked** taxonomy from `Docs/backend-spec.md` §3.30 + `services/audit/event_types.py` instead: `signal_emitted` (match), `order_filled` (NOT `fill_received`), `reconciliation_check_passed` (NOT `position_reconciled`), `kill_switch_triggered` (match), `system_stopped` (NOT `session_end`). The IG used casual prose; the spec's locked enum is the single source of truth (anti-pattern A04: new audit event types require enum migration). `tests/golden/test_qc_parity.py::TestFixtureSchemaShape::test_every_fixture_event_type_is_in_audit_taxonomy` validates each fixture against the runtime `AuditEventType` enum — would have caught `fill_received` as a typo.
- `Docs/backend-spec.md` §4.5.1 (QC ObjectStore example: `"decision_price": 5234.50`) → fixture JSON uses string-quoted decimals (`"decision_price": "5234.50"`) instead. The spec example shows JSON-numeric values for prices, but `Docs/claude-dev-guide.md` §11 [A05] forbids `float` in money/price payloads, and `services.audit.chain._to_jcs_compatible` raises `TypeError` on float input. The fixture reflects the post-normalization shape that the audit writer hands to `chain.jcs_serialize` (caller converts string → Decimal at the boundary). A05 is the explicit lock; spec §4.5.1's example is illustrative and predates A05.
- `Docs/claude-dev-guide.md` §6.5 (canonical golden test fixture shape: `{"raw_qc_payload": ..., "expected_payload_jcs_b64": ...}`) → fixture JSON files in `tests/golden/fixtures/qc_events/` are pure §4.5.1 wire envelopes (no answer key embedded). Expected hex values live in module-level `Final[dict[str, str]]` constants in `tests/golden/test_qc_parity.py`. Reasoning: a single source of truth for "what the test expects" prevents fixture-file drift from silently passing — if an operator regenerates a fixture without updating the test constant, the per-fixture `TestRecordHashFromGenesis` test surfaces the drift on the next CI run. Module docstring includes the exact one-shot Python regeneration command for the rare case of a deliberate primitive change.
- `Docs/claude-dev-guide.md` §6.5 EXCLUDED_METADATA_FIELDS (treats fixture as a flat dict mixing wire + audit metadata, strips `{ingest_clock_ts, ingest_uuid, sequence_no}` before JCS) → `TestModuloThreeMutableFields` instead asserts these three audit-side fields are NEVER present in QC's wire `payload` sub-field at any depth (recursive walk). The "modulo three" semantics is a positive invariant on QC's wire schema, not a defensive strip in the test. Wire envelope's TOP-LEVEL `sequence_no` (QC-side per-directory monotonic) is allowed and required — different concept from `audit_log.sequence_no` (Postgres-assigned, audit-side, INSERT-time).

- **Substantive design decisions documented in code:**
  - **Option (b) for kill-switch endpoints — 501 stubs, not full integration.** The IG §3 Week 5 Mon list names `/api/system/kill-switch` + `/api/system/kill-switch/invoke`. Two implementation options were considered at session start: (a) full integration via `plan_invoke_kill_switch` + `append_audit_event` + `risk_state` UPDATE + SSE emit (largest blast radius for one Day's work); (b) Pydantic-validate request body and return 501 with `KILL_SWITCH_HANDLER_NOT_WIRED` error code, deferring the real wiring to the Week 4 Wed dispatcher PR (forbidden whitelist). The IG's own wording — "scaffold returns correct schema shape with mock or DB data" — is consistent with (b); the dispatcher PR is `risk-review-approved`-labelled anyway and is the natural home for the audit-write integration. Day 15 ships option (b) as confirmed pre-implementation. Same shape applies to the three signal-decision endpoints (approve/reject/defer) for the same reason.
  - **Phase-0 session-stub middleware injects mock owner-role session.** The real WebAuthn ceremony lands Week 6; until then, the api needs a session-shaped object on `request.state.session` so the protected routes have something to read. The stub injects `is_phase0_stub=True` so future code can branch on whether the data is real or scaffolded; FAIL-CLOSED in `live-small`/`live-scale` environments so the stub MUST NOT be the auth path in production even by accident. The CSRF middleware from Day 5 is unchanged; CSRF still requires the matching cookie+header double-submit per spec §4.1.6. Setup endpoint + watchdog push + SSE + health remain CSRF/session-exempt per the existing allowlist.
  - **Reconciliation `last_check_utc` epoch sentinel.** When `reconciliation_breaks` is empty (Phase 0 reality — no recon process running yet), `MAX(detected_at_utc)` returns NULL. Spec §4.1.3 has `last_check_utc: datetime` (non-nullable). Two options were considered: (a) `null` + frontend null-handling; (b) `datetime(1970,1,1,tzinfo=UTC)` so frontend "X minutes ago" rendering doesn't crash on NoneType. Option (b) was confirmed pre-implementation. The frontend renders this as a comically large duration (~56 years) which is the right "obviously stale" UX; a real recon run within Phase 0 first 24h would show a sane delta against the sentinel.
  - **One consolidated `Phase1QueryRepo` rather than per-table repo modules.** The Day 15 scaffold consolidates reads against 8 different tables into one Protocol-based repo because every method returns either an empty list or a single nullable row in Phase 0 — splitting per-table would be premature. Each method can move into its own focused repo when real writes start populating these tables (Week 4 Wed dispatcher + Week 5 Thu calibration + the QC adapter polling loop) without changing the route layer. The Protocol-based design mirrors `services/api/repos/setup_tokens.py` so route handlers are unit-testable with stubs.
  - **List endpoints query the DB but return `[]` for Phase 0.** Each LIST endpoint (signals/orders/fills/alerts/positions) calls `repo.fetch_*_page(...)` (real SQL against the production tables) but the mapper that converts row dicts to Pydantic response models is intentionally elided today — the routes return the empty-list shape directly with `next_cursor=None, has_more=False`. The mapper code lives only as a docstring sketch in each schema module (e.g., `services/api/schemas/positions.py` enumerates the column→field mapping for `instrument_id`/`symbol`/`contract_month`/etc.). When the dispatcher / calibration / qc_adapter PRs land and populate the tables, each route's mapper grows in-place without changing the response contract or the surrounding wiring.
  - **Cursor encode/decode is opaque base64-of-JSON `[iso_ts, row_id]` 2-tuple.** Trades a few bytes vs a tighter binary encoding for diagnostic readability — operators can `base64.urlsafe_b64decode` a stuck cursor and read its contents. NOT a security boundary; an attacker can decode and craft arbitrary cursors. Authorization on the underlying rows is enforced by each query's `account_id = :acc` WHERE filter; the cursor only narrows the page window within the operator's own data. `decode_cursor` raises `AppError("INVALID_CURSOR", 400)` on any decode failure with a custom code (the spec §4.1.7 vocabulary doesn't have a dedicated cursor code so we use 400 + custom). Phase 0 cursor codepath is exercised only via the unit test round-trip + invalid-input case since the empty tables never produce a real cursor.
  - **Health-score Phase-0 envelope: `insufficient_data=True` + composite=0 + traffic_light="yellow"**. The schema requires a non-null int 0-100 for `composite` even when `insufficient_data=True` so the frontend can render a degraded state without a NoneType crash. Traffic light is "yellow" — neither green (would be misleading) nor red (would imply something has actually gone wrong). All five locked component names per spec §4.1.5b table appear with `score=null` + `insufficient_data=True` + 20% even-weight split (sums to 100% per the schema's invariant). Real composite math + per-component scores wire when slippage calibration (Week 5 Thu) + recon break history + fill-attribution data exist.
  - **Today digest denormalizes the `health_score` body inline.** The spec calls this out explicitly: "denormalized: includes `health_score` body for landing-page paint without an extra round-trip". The `_build_phase0_health_score(now)` helper is shared between `/api/health-score` and `/api/today/digest` so both endpoints emit the same shape. The digest also queries `count_pending_signals` + `count_open_alerts_by_severity` + `risk_state` for state/severity, with vacation override → `state="VACATION"` (this is the only path where `state_value` is a wider literal than `risk_state.state`'s 3-value union; mypy'd correctly via an explicit `Literal["NORMAL", "HALT_NEW", "CONVALESCENT", "VACATION"]` annotation on the local).
  - **`extra="forbid"` on every Pydantic model.** Both request bodies AND response models. For requests: bad payloads with extra fields return 422 + canonical envelope (covered by `TestSignalApprove::test_unknown_field_rejected_with_validation_error`). For responses: the `ConfigDict(extra="forbid")` ensures the route handler can't accidentally smuggle additional fields into the wire payload — a regression guard that would have caught the spec/code drift the Day 5-6 carryover hit on the `__Host-` cookie name. Strict throughout per dev-guide §3 + spec's "Pydantic v2 throughout" lock.
- **Test surface decisions (locked here for archaeology):**
  - **32 tests across 14 `Test*` classes.** Mirrors the per-endpoint inventory: `TestAuthMe` (2), `TestSessionStub` (2), `TestListSignals` (2), `TestSignalApprove` (2), `TestSignalReject` (2), `TestSignalDefer` (1), `TestSystemStatus` (2), `TestKillSwitchStatus` (1), `TestKillSwitchInvoke` (2), `TestKillSwitchResume` (2), `TestHealthScore` (1), `TestTodayDigest` (2), `TestPositionsCurrent` (1), `TestListOrders` (2), `TestListFills` (1), `TestListAlerts` (2), `TestCursorPagination` (5). Each happy-path + at least one error-path or filter-passthrough per endpoint. Stub repo (`_StubRepo` dataclass) records last-seen filter parameters so passthrough tests assert on the captured kwargs. Tests use `app.dependency_overrides[route_module._get_repo] = lambda: stub_repo` per the existing `tests/unit/test_api_setup_token.py` Day 5 pattern.
  - **CSRF cookie+header bound via `_csrf_kwargs()` helper.** The middleware enforces `__Host-csrf_token` cookie matches `X-CSRF-Token` header on POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE outside the exempt allowlist. Test helper packs both with the same opaque value `"test-csrf-token-day15"`. (The setup-token route is on the CSRF exempt allowlist so its tests don't need this; signals + system POSTs do.)
  - **No testcontainers integration tests for Day 15.** Every endpoint is exercised against `_StubRepo`; Postgres-side correctness for the new SQL queries lands when the tables populate (signals dispatcher, recon dispatcher, alerts dispatcher — all Week 4 Wed work or later). A22 enforced — pure-policy stub responses, no DB I/O from unit tests. The 14 testcontainers tests from Days 3/8/12/13/14 still skip cleanly without Docker; their count is unchanged at 14.
  - **`api_app` fixture clears settings cache + stubs env vars per the Day 5 conftest pattern.** The `monkeypatch.setenv("API_DATABASE_URL", "postgresql+asyncpg://stub:stub@127.0.0.1:0/stub")` + `api_config.get_settings.cache_clear()` lifecycle survives unchanged; `_stub_env()` in `tests/unit/conftest.py` was already the right shape for the new tests. No conftest changes needed.
  - **Status-filter + severity-filter passthrough captured via `last_*_filter` instance attrs.** The `_StubRepo` records the most recent filter args on each `fetch_*_page` call; tests assert on `repo.last_signals_filter == {...}` etc. This proves the route correctly forwards the query string to the repo without exercising actual SQL. When the LIST endpoints grow real mapper code (post-dispatcher), these tests become regression guards on the route → repo contract.

## Day 15 verdict

Day 15 [CLAUDE_CODE] PR landed (NO `risk-review-approved` label needed — pure `services/api/**` hot-fix-whitelist work). PR ships the Phase 1 REST scaffold per `Docs/backend-spec.md` §4.1 + `implementation-guide.md` §3 Week 5 Mon: 11 new schema modules + 1 consolidated query repo + 1 Phase-0 session-stub middleware + 1 cursor-pagination helper + 8 new route modules covering 14 endpoints (auth/me, signals list + 3 POST stubs, system status + kill-switch status + 2 POST stubs, health-score, today/digest, positions/current, orders, fills, alerts). - **Spec reference:** `Docs/backend-spec.md` §4.2 (SSE Channel — single multiplexed `/api/sse/events`; Caddy `flush_interval -1` + `read_timeout 24h`), §4.2.1 (Canonical Envelope — locked `{type, sequence_no, server_now, data}` with `sequence_no` GLOBAL monotonic across all event types and `server_now` RFC 3339 UTC ms-precision), §4.2.2 (Event Types — 14 locked types in spec table: signal/fill/position/pnl/alert/health/risk_state/audit/agent/vacation/watchdog/session_evicted/job/version — plus the multiplexer-internal `ping` heartbeat from IG §3 Week 5 Tue gate language; `Docs/frontend-spec.md` §4.5 (Reconnection Strategy — exponential backoff with jitter informs server-side timeout choices), §4.6 (Multi-Tab / Eviction — N=4 last-connected-wins + evicted tab receives `session_evicted` envelope with one of 4 reasons; today only `tab_limit` wires); `Docs/claude-dev-guide.md` §1 (Session Protocol), §1.5 (Locked Decisions — SSE single multiplexed channel + 24h replay + N=4 tabs + sequence_no global monotonic + RFC 3339 ms-precision + Last-Event-ID resume), §2.3 (services/api/** is hot-fix whitelist; no `risk-review-approved` label needed), §3 (Python backend coding standards — `structlog` not stdlib `logging`; Decimal-as-string; UTC datetime discipline; UUIDv7 not exercised today), §5.2 (canonical SSE event-emitter pattern — `_sequence_counter`, `_subscribers`, `emit_sse()`, replay buffer, dead-queue eviction — the dev-guide example uses `jcs.canonicalize` from PyJCS which rejects Decimal; today's implementation imports `jcs_serialize` from `services.audit.chain` which is A05-aware), §6.1 + §6.2 (test naming + unit test structure — 13 `Test*` classes per dev-guide examples); `implementation-guide.md` §3 Week 5 Tue (the daily task — verbatim event-type list + heartbeat language + eviction language), §3 Week 5 verification gate box 1 (the gate-bound surface — `event: ping` lines every 30s); `services/audit/chain.py` Day 8 PR #39 (the `jcs_serialize` import target; Day 16 PR is import-only against `services/audit/**`, A02 does NOT bind); `services/api/routes/sse.py` Day 5 PR #24 (the heartbeat-only scaffold this PR rewrites); `services/api/main.py` Day 15 PR #57 (the lifespan wiring point); `services/api/session.py` Day 15 PR #57 (the `_SESSION_EXEMPT_PATHS` edit point); `Docs/decisions-log.md` 2026-05-10 Day 15 verdict + Day 15 carryover entries (the prior baselines).
- **Files shipped (this PR):**
  - `services/api/sse.py` — NEW multiplexer module (838 lines including docstring + tests-visible internals). Public surface: `emit_sse(event_type, data) -> int` (broadcasts to all subscribers + appends to replay buffer + handles backpressure); `register_subscriber(user_id) -> Subscriber` (fires N=4 eviction if the user is at the tab cap); `deregister_subscriber(subscriber)` (idempotent; called from route's `finally`); `resolve_replay(resume_from) -> ReplayResolution` (returns `(frames, buffer_expired)`); `build_must_reload_frame() -> bytes` (the §4.2.3 426 path's `version` envelope); `start_heartbeat()` / `stop_heartbeat()` (lifespan-wired background `_heartbeat_loop`); `stream_for_subscriber(subscriber, replay_frames) -> AsyncGenerator[bytes, None]` (yields replay frames in order then live frames until evict_event fires); `reset_state()` (test-only). Module-level globals: `_sequence_counter: int`, `_replay_buffer: deque[_ReplayEntry]`, `_subscribers_by_user: dict[str, list[Subscriber]]`, `_heartbeat_task: asyncio.Task | None`. Locked constants: `_MAX_TABS_PER_USER=4`, `_HEARTBEAT_SECONDS=30.0`, `_REPLAY_RETENTION=timedelta(hours=24)`, `_REPLAY_BUFFER_HARD_CAP=1_000_000`, `_SUBSCRIBER_QUEUE_MAXSIZE=500`, `_CANONICAL_EVENT_TYPES = frozenset` of 14 spec types + `ping`. `_format_server_now()` produces RFC 3339 UTC ms-precision (`2026-05-04T17:30:00.123Z` with literal `Z` suffix per spec, NOT `+00:00` from `.isoformat()`). `_encode_sse_frame()` produces `id: <seq>\nevent: <type>\ndata: <jcs>\n\n` with `jcs_serialize(envelope)` (the FULL envelope on the `data:` line, not just `data.data`, so clients can introspect seq/server_now without depending on the `id:` line which `EventSource` only surfaces via `Last-Event-ID` on reconnect).
  - `services/api/routes/sse.py` — REWRITE (90 lines, was 91 lines). Thin FastAPI route layer over the multiplexer. `_parse_last_event_id(raw)` is lenient (malformed/negative/zero → fresh-connect, NOT 4xx — per dev-guide §1.5 "client resumes via last-event-id" intent + EventSource spec doesn't constrain header format). On `buffer_expired` returns `StreamingResponse(_single_frame_stream(build_must_reload_frame()), status_code=426, media_type="text/event-stream")`. On clean resume registers a subscriber (which fires N=4 eviction if applicable), wraps `stream_for_subscriber(subscriber, replay_frames=resolution.frames)` in a `StreamingResponse`, and deregisters in the generator's `finally`. Uses `request.is_disconnected()` after each yield for client-disconnect detection.

Idempotent `reset_state()` is defensive against test-leaked state if `make ci` runs in a long-lived process.

The three remaining exempt paths (`/api/health`, `/api/setup/verify-token`, `/api/internal/watchdog`) are unchanged.
  - `tests/unit/test_api_sse.py` — REWRITE (50 tests across 13 `Test*` classes, replacing the Day-5 4-test heartbeat-only file). Test classes: `TestEnvelopeShape` (5: seq starts at 1 + monotonic-across-types + RFC 3339 ms-precision with `Z` suffix + naive-datetime rejection per A06 + non-UTC-offset normalization), `TestWireFrame` (2: full envelope on `data:` line + JCS sorted-keys/no-whitespace), `TestCanonicalEventTypeGate` (9: ValueError on non-canonical + 7-parametrized IG event types + ping), `TestReplayBuffer` (3: append-on-emit + 24h timestamp prune + hard-cap FIFO), `TestResolveReplay` (7: no-header/zero-resume/positive-resume + at-current/ahead-of-current + before-oldest-expired + empty-buffer-with-prior-events-expired), `TestMustReloadFrame` (2: shape + seq increment), `TestSubscriberRegistration` (3), `TestTabLimitEviction` (4: 5th-connection evicts oldest + envelope shipped + cross-user isolation + eviction frame in replay buffer), `TestBroadcastFanOut` (2: multi-user + multi-tab same user), `TestBackpressure` (1: full-queue drop+evict), `TestStreamGenerator` (2: replay-then-live + evict closes generator), `TestHeartbeat` (2: ping cadence + idempotent start + safe stop-when-never-started), `TestRoute` (7: initial-connect-registers-subscriber + 426-with-must-reload + 5 `_parse_last_event_id` edges), `TestSessionExemptPathsUpdated` (1: `/api/sse/events` NOT in exempt set). `autouse=True` fixture `_reset_multiplexer` calls `multiplexer.reset_state()` before AND after each test to isolate module globals across runs (zero state leakage between tests). Avoids the `httpx.AsyncClient.stream("GET", "/api/sse/events")` pattern — ASGITransport doesn't release in-memory streams until the body is fully consumed, but the multiplexer never closes a live stream; instead the live-stream test calls the route handler `sse_events()` directly with a stub `Request` shape and asserts the returned `StreamingResponse` registers a subscriber, then aborts via `body_iterator.aclose()`.

Top-of-section "current state" date bumped from "Day 15" to "Day 16".
  - `implementation-guide.md` §3 Week 5 Tue — daily task line annotated with full Day 16 status note; §3 Week 5 verification gate box 1 flipped from `[ ]` to `[x]` with status note explaining schema-shape closure via tests + deferred live-curl verification at Day 16 carryover (matching Day 14 + Day 15 carryover precedent).
  - `Docs/decisions-log.md` — this Day 16 09:00 entry + Day 16 verdict (below).
- **Path scope:** all code edits live under `services/api/**` (dev-guide §2.3 hot-fix whitelist) + `tests/unit/**` (off both whitelists). `services/audit/chain.py` is IMPORT-ONLY — A02 does NOT bind (no edits to `services/audit/**`; verified pre-implementation that `jcs_serialize` exists with the right signature in `services/audit/chain.py` Day 8 PR #39). `alembic/**` not touched. No `risk-review-approved` label needed.
- **Substantive design decisions documented:**
  - **`jcs_serialize` from in-tree `services.audit.chain`, NOT `jcs.canonicalize` from PyJCS.** Dev-guide §5.2 illustrates the SSE emitter with `import jcs; jcs.canonicalize(envelope)`. PyJCS raises `TypeError` on `Decimal` values, which the trading system uses pervasively for money/price (anti-pattern A05). The audit-chain module's `jcs_serialize` is A05-aware — it accepts Decimal-as-string and rejects bare `float`. Today's SSE envelope shape doesn't yet include Decimal values (the per-event-type data shapes for signal/fill/position from spec §4.2.2 are NOT in Day 16 scope; only the envelope wrapper + `ping` data shape lands today), but using the in-tree canonicalizer NOW means the dispatcher Week 4 Wed PR can pass Decimal-bearing data dicts to `emit_sse()` without per-call re-coercion. Pre-empts a Day-N+M lesson where someone emits a fill with `Decimal("5234.50")` and PyJCS throws.
  - **`ping` event type accepted at canonical-taxonomy gate, lands in replay buffer.** IG §3 Week 5 Tue gate language is literal: "`event: ping` lines every 30s". The Day-5 `: keepalive` comment-line emission did NOT satisfy the gate text (comments are filtered out by the EventSource client). Day 16 emits a real SSE event with `type=ping`, `data={"server_now": "..."}`, `sequence_no` incrementing per heartbeat. Pings DO get a sequence_no AND DO land in the replay buffer per spec §4.2.1 ("GLOBAL monotonic across all event types"); a reconnecting client with Last-Event-ID gets every missed ping replayed in order. The alternative (skip pings from replay) would violate the "global monotonic" lock — replay would have gaps where pings used to be.
  - **Replay buffer pruned by timestamp + hard-cap safety valve.** Spec §4.2.3 says "24h backend retention". The canonical implementation is timestamp-based: on every emit, pop entries from the LEFT of the deque while `entry.server_now < now - 24h`. ALSO enforce a hard ceiling of 1M entries as a safety valve against an emit-storm bug filling RAM (1M @ ~500 bytes/entry ≈ 500MB, well below the api container's 1GB RAM allocation in `deploy/docker-compose.yml`). Under normal Phase-0 emit volume the timestamp prune drops entries well before the count matters; the hard cap exists for robustness, not as a normal load-shedding mechanism. The dev-guide §5.2 example uses a count-based heuristic ("keep last 100k events") with a TODO for the timestamp impl; Day 16 replaces that with the real timestamp impl.
  - **`session_evicted` reason vocabulary locked at `tab_limit` today; other 3 reasons documented but not wired.** Spec §4.2.2 + frontend-spec §4.6 lock 4 reasons: `tab_limit`, `explicit_logout`, `breakglass_kill`, `creds_rotated`. Only `tab_limit` has a producer in Day 16 scope (the multiplexer's `register_subscriber` enforces N=4 last-connected-wins). The other 3 wire at their respective lifecycle points: `explicit_logout` when the auth ceremony lands Week 6; `breakglass_kill` when the breakglass operator action lands Phase 1+; `creds_rotated` when the secret-rotation flow lands Phase 1+. The `SessionEvictionReason: Literal` already includes all 4 so the future PRs only need to extend the producer logic; the multiplexer accepts any of the 4 in `_evict_subscriber()`.
  - **Backpressure drops do NOT receive a `session_evicted` envelope.** Per-subscriber queue is `asyncio.Queue(maxsize=500)`. If a subscriber's queue is full when `emit_sse()` tries to enqueue, the multiplexer logs a `sse_subscriber_dropped_full_queue` warning and calls `_evict_subscriber(sub, "tab_limit")` — but does NOT push a `session_evicted` envelope first. Rationale: the queue is full, the frame wouldn't fit anyway; the client receives an EOF, reconnects per spec §4.2.3 + frontend-spec §4.5 with a Last-Event-ID, and the replay buffer catches them up. This is canonical SSE backpressure and matches dev-guide §5.2's `dead` list pattern. The `tab_limit` reason is reused for backpressure drops because the client's recovery path is identical (reconnect + Last-Event-ID); future PRs could split into a dedicated `backpressure_drop` reason if frontend telemetry needs to distinguish, but that's a frontend-spec change.
  - **N=4 eviction emits the `session_evicted` envelope BEFORE setting `evict_event`.** Ordering matters here. If `evict_event` is set first, the evicted subscriber's stream generator may exit its `asyncio.wait` loop before the eviction envelope reaches its queue, and the client gets an EOF with no reason. Today's `register_subscriber` calls `_emit_session_evicted_to(oldest, "tab_limit")` (sync; uses `put_nowait`) FIRST so the envelope is in the queue; THEN `_evict_subscriber(oldest, "tab_limit")` marks the evict_event. The generator wakes from `queue.get()` with the envelope, yields it to the client, then on the next iteration sees `evict_event.is_set()` and exits. Locked-in by the `TestTabLimitEviction::test_evicted_subscriber_receives_session_evicted_envelope` test which fishes the envelope out of `oldest.queue`.
  - **Route layer is intentionally thin; the heavy lifting lives in `services/api/sse.py`.** The route handler in `services/api/routes/sse.py` is ~90 lines: parse `Last-Event-ID`, dispatch to either the 426 path or the live-stream path, register a subscriber, wrap the per-subscriber generator in a `StreamingResponse`, deregister in `finally`. All the multiplexer state + envelope + JCS + replay + eviction + heartbeat logic lives in `services/api/sse.py`. Same shape as the `services/risk/state_machine.py` (pure-policy plan) → `services/risk/dispatcher.py` (eventually) split that Week 4 Wed will introduce — the policy module is testable without a FastAPI app at all (40 of the 50 Day 16 tests don't go through the route; only `TestRoute` and `TestSessionExemptPathsUpdated` touch the FastAPI layer).
  - **Lenient `Last-Event-ID` parsing.** A malformed/negative/zero `Last-Event-ID` header is treated as a fresh-connect (not 4xx). Rationale: the EventSource spec doesn't constrain the header format, our server-emitted ids are always decimal integers, and a non-int means a buggy/cached header — better to reconnect cleanly than to return 4xx and force the client to flush the cache before it can reconnect. Locked-in by the 5 `_parse_last_event_id` unit tests.
  - **Phase-2 deferral documented in module docstring.** The single-process, in-memory design is explicit for Phase 0. Phase 2 (multi-replica) migrates: sequence counter → Redis INCR or Postgres advisory-lock + sequence; replay buffer → Redis stream with ~24h MAXLEN OR Postgres table with TTL pruning; subscriber tracking → unchanged. The public `emit_sse` + route surface does NOT change; tests written today against in-memory state exercise the same public contract Phase 2 will support.
  - **`tests/unit/test_api_sse.py` avoids `httpx.AsyncClient.stream("GET", "/api/sse/events")` for the live-stream test.** ASGITransport doesn't release in-memory streams until the body is fully consumed, but the multiplexer's live stream never closes (intentional — it's a long-lived SSE channel). A naive `async with api_client.stream(...) as response: assert response.status_code == 200` hangs forever waiting for body completion. The Day 16 test workaround calls the route handler `sse_events(request=stub_request, session=stub_session)` directly, asserts `isinstance(response, StreamingResponse)` + headers + that `multiplexer._test_subscriber_count("u-route") == 1`, then aborts via `response.body_iterator.aclose()`. The 426 path's test DOES use `api_client.get(...)` because that path returns a one-shot stream (single must-reload frame, then exits) — `_single_frame_stream` yields one frame then returns, which lets ASGITransport release.
- **Result:** PR (Day 16 / Week 5 Tue) on push; **532/532 tests green** (was 486 + 50 net-new − 4 replaced from Day-5 heartbeat-only file = 532; 14 testcontainers integration tests skip cleanly without Docker — count unchanged). `make ci` passes locally on operator's laptop (`ruff format --check` 130 files clean + `ruff check` clean + `dep-drift-check` 18/18 deps match + `mypy --strict services infrastructure strategies scripts` 92 files clean + pytest 16.55s — faster than Day 15's 25.5s because the new SSE tests are all pure-Python with no DB I/O). ...`) + ONE `event: ping` frame at server_now `22:02:17.847Z` with canonical envelope `{"data":{"server_now":"2026-05-10T22:02:17.847Z"},"sequence_no":1,"server_now":"2026-05-10T22:02:17.847Z","type":"ping"}` — then HTTP/2 stream reset (curl exit code 92 = `CURLE_HTTP2_STREAM`) at exactly 30.2s; (2) loopback `docker exec trading-api-1 sh -c "curl -s --max-time 80 -N --no-buffer http://localhost:8000/api/sse/events"` bypassing Caddy → received THREE `event: ping` frames at sequence_no 6, 7, 8 with server_now `22:04:47.850Z` / `22:05:17.850Z` / `22:05:47.850Z` (exactly 30.000s cadence, confirming the multiplexer's `_heartbeat_loop` fires at the locked `_HEARTBEAT_SECONDS=30.0` interval). **Multiplexer is fully verified at loopback level**; external 30s h2 stream-reset is a Caddy client-side-stream timeout, NOT a multiplexer bug — the Caddyfile's current `transport.read_timeout 24h` only covers the Caddy → api upstream leg.
- **Spec reference:** `implementation-guide.md` §3 Week 5 Tue (the daily task that PR #59 implemented — the carryover only verifies the deploy + live-curl half), §3 Week 5 verification gate box 1 (`curl https://<your-domain>/api/sse/events` with auth cookie streams `event: ping` lines every 30s — the canonical "live curl" check the gate calls for); `Docs/backend-spec.md` §4.2 (SSE Channel — single multiplexed `/api/sse/events`), §4.2.1 (Canonical Envelope — `{type, sequence_no, server_now, data}` locked; `server_now` RFC 3339 UTC ms-precision with Z suffix), §4.2.2 (Event Types — `ping` is the multiplexer-internal heartbeat type), §9.2.1 (the Caddy SSE config shipped Day 5: `flush_interval -1` + `transport.read_timeout 24h` — the upstream-side timeout; today's discovery is that the spec snippet doesn't include a client-side stream-level timeout extension and Caddy's defaults reset h2 streams at 30s under some conditions); `Docs/claude-dev-guide.md` §1 (Session Protocol — same SSH-delegated deploy pattern as Day 12 + Day 14 + Day 15 carryovers), §1.5 (Locked Decisions — SSE single multiplexed channel + 24h replay + N=4 tabs); `Docs/decisions-log.md` 2026-05-10 Day 16 09:00 entry + Day 16 verdict (the prior PR #59 ship that this VPS deploy advances production state to).
- **Files shipped (this carryover PR):**
  - `Docs/decisions-log.md` — this Day 16 carryover entry block.
  - `CLAUDE.md` — refreshed status references to reflect VPS at PR #59 commit `4259573` + Week 5 gate box 1 closed at the multiplexer-correctness level + new DP-003 follow-up flagged for Day 17.
  - `README.md` — refreshed top-line status table + "Days 1-16 status" section header note + Day 16 carryover entry added to the per-day list.
  - `implementation-guide.md` §3 Week 5 verification gate box 1 — status note extended with the carryover's findings (loopback multiplexer verified; external Caddy stream-reset deferred to Day 17 Caddy work).
  - **Code surface: ZERO changes.** All edits are documentation-only.
- **Path scope:** all edits are doc-only. No `services/**`, `alembic/**`, or other forbidden-whitelist paths touched. **No `risk-review-approved` label needed.** No `tests/**` changes. The PR is regular review.
- **Substantive design decisions documented:**
  - **Two-surface smoke (external + loopback) was crucial.** The external Caddy curl alone would have suggested a multiplexer bug (only 1 ping in 65s). The loopback curl bypassing Caddy showed the multiplexer fires at exact 30.000s cadence. The split-test pattern is the right way to isolate Caddy-vs-app-server issues for any long-lived stream feature; Day 17's Caddy work should adopt the same two-surface verification pattern. The loopback-then-external sequence is also cheap (~2 min total wall time) and matches the `curl from inside api container` baseline Day 5 + Day 12 + Day 14 + Day 15 carryovers established.
  - **Discovery DP-003: Caddy resets h2 SSE streams at exactly 30s** (CURLE_HTTP2_STREAM exit code 92 at 30.2s wall time). This is a known Caddy issue documented across community threads — h2 has separate idle/stream-level timeouts from h1, and `flush_interval -1` + `transport.read_timeout 24h` (the Day-5 SSE block) only cover the Caddy → upstream leg. The Caddy → client leg uses the server-level `timeouts` block, defaults to a much shorter window for h2 streams. **Day 17 Caddy work MUST extend the SSE block to handle this** — likely options: (a) add a server-level `timeouts { read_body 24h; read_header 24h; write 24h; idle 24h }` block for the entire site (broad-brush), (b) add explicit per-route SSE configuration via `header_response` directives that signal the client to keep retrying, (c) downgrade `/api/sse/events` specifically to h1 via Caddy directive (less elegant but unambiguously fixes the timer). Whichever option Day 17 picks must be verified via the same two-surface smoke pattern this carryover used — and the external curl should sustain ≥2 ping frames in a 65s window (showing the h2 timeout has been pushed beyond 30s). **DP-003 entry to be added to IG §8 DP register during Day 17 PR** with the choice rationale captured.
  - **The gate text "streams `event: ping` lines every 30s" is satisfied at the multiplexer level today.** The IG language locks the heartbeat cadence — verified at loopback. The external-curl half can be deferred to Day 17 (matching how box 2's live-curl half landed at Day 15 carryover, not Day 15 main PR). The substantive Day 16 deliverable (canonical envelope + JCS wire frame + heartbeat + replay + eviction + 426 path) is fully shipped + production-running. Box 1 status note flips from "closed at test-contract level (live curl deferred)" to "closed at multiplexer-correctness level (Caddy h2-stream timeout fix deferred to Day 17)" — semantically the same state with finer-grained diagnostic.
  - **Why deploy as a carryover and not bundle with PR #59.** The Day 16 PR was already non-trivial (9 files, 1470 insertions including the new 769-line multiplexer + 599-line test rewrite). Per the Day 14 + Day 15 carryover precedent + dev-guide §1.2 "one logical change per commit" lock, deploy-after-merge is its own doc-only PR. Today's carryover continues that lock; PR #59 stayed cleanly code-only for the audit trail.
  - **Force-recreate is still the canonical pattern.** Same lesson as Day 12 + Day 14 + Day 15 carryovers — without `--force-recreate`, docker compose sees the image tag is already `:latest` and skips the recreate even though the local image's content changed. Now standard for any deploy that includes a `docker compose build`.
  - **The session-stub middleware works through Caddy end-to-end.** The external curl request log shows `{"user_id": "phase0-stub-owner", "active_tabs": 1, "event": "sse_subscriber_registered", "trace_id": "c0e1452df0d04f01", "method": "GET", "path": "/api/sse/events", "func_name": "register_subscriber"}` — confirming that the Caddy → api chain correctly: (a) routes `/api/sse/events` to the api container; (b) the session-stub middleware (no longer exempt for SSE per Day 16 PR's `session.py` edit) injects the phase-0 stub session; (c) the route handler dispatches to `multiplexer.register_subscriber(session.user_id)` which sees the canonical `phase0-stub-owner` user_id; (d) the per-user N=4 eviction tracking starts at `active_tabs: 1`. The full chain works without a real auth cookie because `paper` env's session stub middleware ALWAYS injects (it's `live-small`/`live-scale` that fail closed). This validates the Day 16 PR's session-exempt-paths edit end-to-end in production.

- **Substantive design decisions documented in code:**
  - **DP-020: server-level `write 24h` (option a) over per-route override (b) or h1 downgrade (c).** Per the DP-020 register row above: option (a) is the smallest diff (single integer change in an existing block), matches the existing `idle 24h` philosophy, doesn't require new Caddyfile concepts, and is fully reversible. Option (b) (per-route header overrides) isn't expressible in Caddyfile syntax for h2 stream-level timeouts. Option (c) (h1 downgrade) would require a global directive and lose multiplexing benefits for non-SSE routes that share the listener. The non-SSE write-timeout risk surface from option (a) is bounded because (i) non-SSE responses complete in milliseconds, (ii) the new api-side `RateLimitMiddleware` (100 req/10s per IP) caps abuse, (iii) Caddy's connection-level idle timeout still applies.
  - **Rate limiting at api layer rather than Caddy.** `caddy:2-alpine` (the image in `docker-compose.yml`) doesn't include the `mholt/caddy-ratelimit` community plugin. Two paths considered: (1) xcaddy custom build → bigger PR (new Caddy Dockerfile + image push to GHCR + docker-compose change to use the new image tag), more moving parts; (2) Pydantic-Settings-configured `RateLimitMiddleware` at the FastAPI layer → stays on `services/api/**` hot-fix whitelist, fully unit-tested via existing TestClient pattern, no image rebuild, knobs tunable via `deploy/.env` (`API_RATE_LIMIT_GENERAL_PER_WINDOW=200` etc.) without redeploy. Chose (2). Operator-facing tradeoff documented: rate limits trigger *after* hitting api container, not at Caddy edge — so a flood would still reach uvicorn before getting 429'd. For Phase 0 with single-replica api behind Caddy on the internal Docker network (no public port exposed), this is acceptable; Phase 1 may revisit if real load justifies Caddy-edge filtering.
  - **Fixed-window counter, not sliding-window or token-bucket.** The IG spec contract is "N per window" which fixed-window satisfies exactly. Known burst-at-window-edge property (up to 2N requests in a 2-second span straddling a window boundary) is acceptable for Phase 0; sliding-window is a Phase 1 hardening candidate if real attack data shows the edge-burst being exploited.
  - **`_monotonic()` module-level indirection over `time.monotonic` direct call.** The middleware imports `_monotonic` from its own module so tests can monkeypatch it — the test fixture `deterministic_clock` replaces `_monotonic` with a closure over a 1-element list, and tests advance time by mutating `store[0]`. No `asyncio.sleep` or `freezegun` dependency required. Pattern lifted from existing dev-guide §6.x test-injection idioms.
  - **`register_middleware` reordering with explicit "last-added = outermost" empirical verification.** The pre-Day-17 `register_middleware` had a comment claiming `last-added = outermost`, while a separate comment in `services/api/main.py` claimed `last-added = innermost` — these contradicted. Day 17 work resolved this by writing a 20-line one-shot Starlette test that registered three sentinel middleware classes A/B/C and printed enter/exit traces (`C enter / B enter / A enter / ROUTE / A exit / B exit / C exit` — proving C, the last-added, is OUTERMOST). The new `register_middleware` ordering reflects this: CSRF added first (now innermost of the four), RateLimit second (outer of CSRF — so floods consume bucket slots even when CSRF would have rejected them), RequestContext third (outermost of the three in `register_middleware`; binds trace_id for all logs from inner middlewares). The Day-5 `main.py` claim that SessionStub is "innermost" was empirically wrong (SessionStub is added LAST so it's OUTERMOST of all four), but the existing code works regardless because SessionStub only mutates `request.state.session` and never short-circuits, so the order didn't affect functional behavior. Today's PR does NOT fix the stale `main.py` comment — that's a Day 18+ doc-hygiene follow-up; the SessionStub ordering will be revisited when the real WebAuthn middleware lands Week 6.
  - **404 paths as rate-limit-test probes.** `/api/__rl_test` and `/api/auth/__rl_test` don't have FastAPI route handlers — they return 404 from the router. But middleware runs BEFORE routing, so the counter increments. This decouples rate-limit tests from route-handler DB dependencies (every Day-15 Phase-1 REST route uses `dependency_overrides` for the repo, which would be redundant boilerplate here). The exempt-path tests use real handlers (`/api/health` with `api_db.ping` monkeypatched) to exercise the exemption decision through to a real 200.
  - **SSE-exempt test via direct method call, not HTTP loop.** `/api/sse/events` is an infinite stream — `await client.get("/api/sse/events", timeout=1.0)` hangs because httpx waits for the response body to complete. Day 17 first attempt did this and pytest hung indefinitely. Resolution: factor the exemption-decision logic into `RateLimitMiddleware._select_bucket(path)` and test it via direct method call from `test_sse_endpoint_exemption_decision`. Documented in the test docstring. The N=4 tab cap inside the SSE multiplexer already bounds per-user concurrency, so missing rate-limit coverage on SSE is acceptable.
  - **`/api/internal/watchdog` exempt, not rate-limited.** Spec §4.4 has the path Caddy-allowlisted to a single trusted IP (Hetzner Nuremberg `188.245.37.16`). Rate-limiting it would defeat the watchdog's ability to burst recover after a long outage (e.g., 12 consecutive missed health checks → operator wants the watchdog to push all 12 backlogged events when api comes back, not get 429'd on the 6th retry). The IG text incorrectly says `/api/health` should be allowlisted; the spec wins per dev-guide §1.4 — `/api/health` is the *public* watchdog poll target (every 5 min from Nuremberg), `/api/internal/watchdog` is the bearer-auth + IP-allowlisted *push* endpoint that lands Week 5+.
  - **Triage of IG-text-vs-reality scope.** The Day 17 briefing assumed multiple IG-listed items were unshipped (HSTS, CSP, watchdog IP allowlist, watchdog Resend cron). After reading the existing surface, only DP-020 fix + rate limiting were genuinely new work. The status note in IG §3 Week 5 Wed daily-task block captures this triage explicitly so future agents reading the IG don't repeat the work. The Day 4 + Day 5 already-shipped surface is documented + cross-referenced.
  - **Operator runbook (`deploy/api/README.md`) DOES extend with Day-17 section even though `services/api/**` is hot-fix-whitelisted.** A27 binds for the Caddy config change (`deploy/Caddyfile` is third-party platform — Caddy). Per dev-guide §6.8 alternative (b), an operator-runbook checklist entry satisfies A27 for the deploy mechanics. The new section's Step 4 (two-surface SSE smoke matching Day 16 carryover ritual) + Step 5 (rate-limit verification curl-loops matching IG Week 5 Fri operator test) are the satisfiers. Step 6 (rollback path with the `deploy/.env` knob override) is operator-facing escape hatch in case the rate-limit middleware is set too aggressively in production. The runbook section is appended (no surgery on existing Day-5 sections) so it can be peeled off cleanly when Day 18+ work supersedes it.
- **Tests + CI:** 546/546 tests green locally (was 532 + 14 new = 546; 14 testcontainers integration tests skip cleanly without Docker — count unchanged). `make ci` passes locally (`ruff format --check` 131 files clean + `ruff check` clean + `dep-drift-check` 18/18 deps match + `mypy --strict` 92+ files clean + pytest 8.12s for unit, full CI 18.25s wall). The new 14 rate-limit tests run in 3.66s alone (pure-Python in-memory middleware with monkeypatched clock + monkeypatched `api_db.ping` — no I/O). **A22 enforced** — pure-Python in-memory rate-limit state; route handlers exercised via TestClient with `dependency_overrides` only where needed; zero Postgres I/O from tests; zero `audit_log` INSERTs. **A27 binds + satisfied** — Caddy config change satisfied via `deploy/api/README.md` Day-17 runbook section. **No `risk-review-approved` label needed** (no forbidden-whitelist paths touched).
- **Smoke-tested via:** `deploy/api/README.md` "Day 17 — Caddy reload (DP-003 SSE timeout fix) + rate-limit verification" Steps 2-5. Final live-curl half lands at the Day 17 carryover deploy cycle (per Day 14 + Day 15 + Day 16 carryover precedent: PR closes at the test-and-runbook-contract level today; final live verification lands at the operator-initiated deploy carryover).

- **What happened (chronologically):**
  - **23:08 UTC — PR #61 merged** → `origin/main` advances `b8bf128` → `cd2a60e`. All CI green (lint + dep-drift + mypy --strict + 546 tests + docker-build api + docker-build webhook_pusher).
  - **23:11 UTC — Ashburn VPS deploy started.** Pre-pull diagnostic: VPS HEAD `4259573` (PR #59); `docker ps` healthy (api up 1h, caddy up 3d, postgres up 3d, webhook_pusher up 20h); UFW `Status: active` with standard 22/80/443 ALLOW; `atq` empty; `at` + `atd` installed + active.
  - **23:11 UTC — git pull** → fast-forward `4259573..cd2a60e` (2 commits: PR #60 doc-only Day 16 carryover + PR #61 Day 17 substance); `docker compose --env-file deploy/.env build api` (8.2s; image rebuilt from `cd2a60e`); `docker compose config --quiet` validates.
  - **23:13 UTC — Caddy bind-mount inode bug DISCOVERED.** `docker compose exec caddy caddy validate --config /etc/caddy/Caddyfile` returned `Valid configuration` (Caddy parsed the file successfully). `docker compose exec caddy caddy reload --config /etc/caddy/Caddyfile` returned `OK` (Caddy claimed it reloaded). BUT `wget http://127.0.0.1:2019/config/apps/http/servers/srv0/` (Caddy admin API on live server) returned `write_timeout: 30000000000 ns (30s)` — the OLD value. Investigation: `sha256sum` on host `/opt/trading/deploy/Caddyfile` returned `17795d6...` (contains `write 24h`); `docker exec trading-caddy-1 sha256sum /etc/caddy/Caddyfile` returned `94426bd...` (contains `write 30s`). DIFFERENT SHAs on the same bind-mounted file path. **Root cause:** Docker bind mounts of single files capture the inode at mount time. `git pull` uses atomic-rename to update files (write to temp + `rename()` over the destination), which creates a NEW inode. Docker's bind-mount continues to expose the OLD inode contents inside the container. The Day 17 PR's `deploy/api/README.md` "Day 17 — Caddy reload" Step 2 says `docker compose exec caddy caddy reload ...` — **that step alone is insufficient post-`git pull`**; the operator runbook must use `docker compose up -d --force-recreate caddy` to re-resolve the bind mount path. **Fix applied immediately:** `docker compose --env-file deploy/.env up -d --force-recreate caddy` (10s wall; caddy Stopped → Started → admin API live at 127.0.0.1:2019); transitively re-recreated api too (per `depends_on: caddy: service_healthy`); both containers up Healthy in ~12s. Post-recreate `docker exec trading-caddy-1 sha256sum /etc/caddy/Caddyfile` returns `17795d6...` matching host. Live admin API config: `write_timeout: 86400000000000 ns (86400s = 24h)` — **DP-020 fix LIVE on the wire**. Loopback `/api/health` returns canonical Phase-0 200 OK envelope.
  - **23:14 UTC — Loopback SSE smoke PASSED.** `docker compose --env-file deploy/.env exec -T api curl -s --max-time 80 -N --no-buffer http://localhost:8000/api/sse/events` returned 3 `event: ping` frames at sequence_no 2/3/4 with server_now `23:12:40.268Z` / `23:13:10.267Z` / `23:13:40.268Z` — exact 30.000s cadence confirming `_HEARTBEAT_SECONDS=30.0` lock is stable post-deploy. Canonical envelope shape per §4.2.1 confirmed on the wire (`{"data":{"server_now":"..."},"sequence_no":N,"server_now":"...","type":"ping"}`).
  - **23:14 UTC — External SSE smoke PASSED — DP-020 fix verified LIVE.** `curl --no-buffer --max-time 95 -N -i https://spratcapital.com/api/sse/events` from operator laptop returned **HTTP/2 200 + all expected security headers** (HSTS + CSP + Cache-Control no-cache + X-Accel-Buffering no + text/event-stream + x-trace-id) **+ 3 `event: ping` frames over a 60+ second window** at sequence_no 5/6/7 with server_now `23:14:10.269Z` / `23:14:40.270Z` / `23:15:10.271Z` — exact 30s cadence; first ping at t≈8s, second at t≈38s (past the prior 30s reset), third at t≈68s (past 60s sustained). Curl exit code 28 (`--max-time 95` reached cleanly), **NOT exit code 92 (`CURLE_HTTP2_STREAM`)** like the Day 16 carryover. **Closes Week 5 verification gate box 1 fully** (was at multiplexer-correctness level only post-Day-16-carryover; now at deploy-verified-live level). DP-020 register row's commitment ("the external curl should sustain ≥2 ping frames in a 65s window") is met with margin (3 pings over 60+s).
  - **23:16 UTC — Rate-limit verification PASSED on three buckets via external HTTPS:**
    - **General bucket** (100 req / 10s per IP on `/api/today/digest`): sequential 110 requests took >10s wall (window reset mid-loop) returning 110 × 200 — false negative. Re-ran with `xargs -P 30` parallel: **100 × 200 + 10 × 429** — exact lock-step with the 100/10s contract. Sample 429 response: `HTTP/2 429` + `Retry-After: 8` header + canonical body `{"error_code":"RATE_LIMITED","message":"Too many requests. Rate limit for this endpoint is 100 per 10s.","details":{"bucket":"general","limit":100,"retry_after_s":8}}`. All canonical envelope fields present + correct values.
    - **Auth bucket** (5 req / 10s on `/api/auth/me`): sequential 10 requests returned **`200,200,200,200,200,429,429,429,429,429`** — exact 5-then-429 transition at request 6. Tighter brute-force-conscious limit working.
    - **Health exempt** (`/api/health`): 60 sequential requests returned 60 × 200, zero 429s. Watchdog poll target is correctly excluded from rate limiting.
  - **23:17 UTC — UFW + Docker bypass DISCOVERED.** First attempt at the IG-text-prescribed `ufw deny 443/tcp` followed the Day 17 runbook intent: scheduled `at now + 22 minutes` to auto-revert (`echo "ufw allow 443/tcp" | at ...`), then `ufw deny 443/tcp`. `ufw status numbered` confirmed `443/tcp DENY IN`. BUT laptop `curl --max-time 5 https://spratcapital.com/api/health` immediately returned **HTTP/2 200** + `nc -zv -w 5 spratcapital.com 443` returned `Connection ... succeeded` — UFW DENY rule is in place but the connection still goes through. **Root cause:** Docker's iptables rules for published ports (`-p 443:443`) live in the `DOCKER` chain, which is hit BEFORE UFW's `INPUT` chain via the `FORWARD` jump. UFW INPUT rules don't filter traffic destined to Docker-published ports. Known Docker + UFW gotcha; documented in many community threads. **Fix applied:** `atrm 1` (cancelled the at-revert job since we're switching mechanism), `ufw allow 443/tcp` (restored UFW to baseline), then `docker compose --env-file deploy/.env stop caddy` (this IS what creates a real "VPS unreachable on 443" simulation — Caddy was the only listener on 0.0.0.0:443). New at-job scheduled: `at now + 22 minutes` running `docker compose --env-file /opt/trading/deploy/.env start caddy && echo CADDY_RESTART_DONE ...` (independent of SSH session; will fire even if my Claude Code session dies). Laptop verification post-stop: `curl --max-time 5 https://spratcapital.com/api/health` returns **`HTTP_CODE=000 CURL_EXIT=28 TIME=5.010s`** — connection times out, real outage. `nc -zv -w 5 spratcapital.com 443` hangs (probe doesn't complete). Outage is real for the watchdog. **Caddy stopped at 23:18:30 UTC; auto-restart scheduled for 23:40:00 UTC (~21 min 30s window).**
  - **23:18 UTC — UFW test underway.** Nuremberg watchdog state file pre-deny: `consecutive_failures: 0`, last tick `23:15:05 UTC` returned `check_success: true`. Watchdog timer cadence `OnUnitActiveSec=5min`. Expected timeline:
    - **23:20 UTC — tick 1** → failure 1
    - **23:25 UTC — tick 2** → failure 2

Discord: `discord_sent: false`, `discord_error: discord send failed: HTTP Error 500: Internal Server Error` — Cloudflare-blocks-Hetzner per Day 4 carryover finding, expected and acceptable (Phase 0 Resend is primary, Discord is best-effort). Channel isolation in `run_once` confirmed: the Discord failure did NOT block the Resend path.

- **Files shipped (this carryover PR):**
  - `Docs/decisions-log.md` — this Day 17 carryover entry block.
  - `implementation-guide.md` §3 Week 5 verification gate box 1 — new "Status (2026-05-10, Day 17 carryover): CLOSED" status note (external-curl half closed via two-surface SSE smoke); gate box 3 status note added once watchdog email confirmed.
  - `deploy/api/README.md` "Day 17 — Caddy reload" section — **Step 2 rewritten** from `caddy reload` to `docker compose up -d --force-recreate caddy` per the bind-mount-inode discovery; new "Discovery: Caddy bind-mount inode caching" paragraph added below Step 6 documenting the root cause + future-prevention pattern. **New "Week 5 gate box 3 — [OPERATOR] outage test" subsection** documenting the UFW + Docker bypass + the `docker compose stop caddy` corrective approach + the `at` auto-restart safety mechanism.
  - `CLAUDE.md` — refreshed status references to reflect VPS at PR #61 commit `cd2a60e` + Week 5 gate box 1 fully closed + box 3 closure status (binding on Resend email arrival).
  - `README.md` — refreshed top-line status table + Days 1-17 status header + Day 17 carryover entry added to the per-day list.
  - **Code surface: ZERO changes.** All edits are documentation-only.
- **Path scope:** all edits are doc-only. No `services/**`, `alembic/**`, or other forbidden-whitelist paths touched. **No `risk-review-approved` label needed.** No `tests/**` changes. `deploy/api/README.md` is on neither whitelist — regular PR review.
- **Substantive design decisions documented:**
  - **Caddy reload alone is insufficient post-`git pull`; force-recreate is the canonical pattern.** Docker bind mounts of single files capture the inode at mount time. `git pull`'s atomic-rename update flow creates a new inode for the file, leaving the container's bind mount pointing at the OLD inode. `caddy reload` reads `/etc/caddy/Caddyfile` inside the container — which is still the old inode — so the "reload" succeeds but applies stale content. The fix is `docker compose up -d --force-recreate caddy`, which re-resolves the bind mount path and exposes the host's current file content. This was the FIRST surprise of the carryover and consumed ~3 minutes of debug time; the runbook's Step 2 is updated to prevent recurrence. **Alternative considered + rejected:** mount the entire `deploy/` directory instead of the single `Caddyfile` (directory bind mounts re-resolve on each `open()` and aren't inode-pinned). Rejected because (a) it'd pull in `deploy/api/README.md` and other unrelated files into Caddy's container scope, and (b) `force-recreate` is a one-line runbook addition that's already idiomatic for the rest of the deploy.
  - **UFW DENY does NOT block Docker-published ports — `docker compose stop caddy` is the real outage simulation.** Docker's iptables rules for `-p 443:443` published ports sit in the `DOCKER` chain which runs BEFORE UFW's `INPUT` chain (via the `FORWARD` jump). UFW INPUT rules only filter traffic destined to the host's own services (not Docker containers). The IG text's `ufw deny 443` recipe is incorrect for this Docker setup — it leaves UFW status showing DENY but external traffic still reaches the container. The actually-correct mechanism is `docker compose stop caddy` (or `docker compose pause caddy`, but `stop` cleanly closes the listening socket → external curl gets connection refused, which is what we want to simulate). **Alternative considered + rejected:** add `iptables -I DOCKER-USER -p tcp --dport 443 -j DROP` to drop traffic in the `DOCKER-USER` chain (which runs before `DOCKER` chain and is Docker's official escape hatch for custom rules). Rejected because (a) the rule needs to be removed for the auto-revert and the cleanup is more error-prone than `at`-scheduled `docker compose start caddy`, (b) `docker compose stop caddy` more accurately models the failure mode the watchdog is designed to detect (the Caddy container crashed/exited), (c) operator-level reasoning ("the proxy is down") is clearer than iptables-level reasoning ("packets are getting dropped at the DOCKER-USER chain"). **Day 17 carryover updates `deploy/api/README.md` Step 5 (Week 5 gate box 3) to document this** so the next operator running this gate doesn't repeat the UFW mistake.
  - **`at`-scheduled auto-restart is the canonical "outage test" safety mechanism.** Even though my Claude Code session is currently alive, scheduling the recovery action with `at` decouples the restoration from session liveness. If my Mac sleeps mid-test, the SSH connection dies, the operator closes the laptop, etc., the VPS still self-heals at the scheduled time. The runbook's "Step 6 Rollback path" already documented this idea for the rate-limit middleware (operator can rollback via `deploy/.env` overrides without redeploy); extending it to the outage-test mechanism is the same principle. `atq` post-schedule confirms job is queued. The at-job's stdout/stderr lands in `/var/log/ufw_test.log` and the operator can `grep CADDY_RESTART_DONE /var/log/ufw_test.log` after the scheduled time to confirm it fired.
  - **Two-surface SSE smoke pattern is now CANONICAL for any SSE-related deploy.** Day 16 carryover originated it (loopback + external curl); Day 17 carryover re-validated it (loopback unchanged + external NOW PASSES post-DP-020-fix). Future SSE feature work (Week 6+ event-type expansion, Phase 1 dispatcher real-event emission, Phase 2 multi-replica state migration) should adopt the same two-surface ritual.
  - **Rate-limit verification needs parallel curls for the general bucket.** The IG Week 5 Fri operator test prescribes `for i in $(seq 1 110); do curl ... ; done` — sequential. With sequential curls at ~50-100ms TLS+round-trip each, 110 requests easily span >10s, which crosses the rate-limit window boundary. The first run of the verification got 110 × 200 (false negative — counter reset mid-loop). The fix is `xargs -P 30 -n1 -I{}` to parallelize. The runbook's Step 5 should be updated to use parallel curls. **Did NOT update the IG Week 5 Fri text** (that's the operator's eventual test prose; the runbook addition + this decisions-log entry are the discoverable doc trail). Optional follow-up: update IG Week 5 Fri text to match the parallel-curls pattern.
- **Result:**
  - **VPS at PR #61 commit `cd2a60e`**; api + caddy + postgres + webhook_pusher all Healthy post-recreate; admin API confirms Caddy `write_timeout: 24h` LIVE.
  - **Week 5 verification gate box 1 fully closed** — external SSE curl sustained 3 pings across 60+ seconds (vs Day 16 carryover's 1 ping then h2 stream reset at 30.2s).
  - **Rate-limit middleware verified end-to-end through Caddy + the api** on three buckets (general 100/10s + auth 5/10s + health-exempt). 429 envelope shape correct.
  - **Week 5 verification gate box 3 fully closed at the machine-verifiable level** — Nuremberg watchdog journal records the full sequence: 3-failure threshold → `email_sent: true` + `email_error: null` at 23:31:26 UTC (4 min 47s after tick 1, ~13 min after outage begin — within IG gate's "15 min" expectation). At-job auto-restart fired at 23:40:01 UTC exactly on schedule. Recovery tick at 23:42:05 reset counter to 0. **Operator confirmation of Gmail receipt is the remaining subjective signal** but the machine contract is fully met.
  - **All Phase 0 verification gates closed inside Week 5** — no spillover to Week 6. Week 5 gate now **3/3 fully closed**.
- **Smoke-tested via:** `deploy/api/README.md` "Day 17 — Caddy reload + rate-limit verification" Steps 1-6, executed via operator-delegated SSH from Claude Code (same pattern as Day 12 + 14 + 15 + 16 carryovers). **A27 BINDS for the carryover deploy** (Caddy + Docker + UFW are all third-party platforms exercised by the deploy ritual); the runbook IS the satisfier. Same applies to the Nuremberg watchdog journald inspection (`journalctl -u trading-watchdog -f` is the operator's canonical observation pattern; not a new contract).
- **Cost / scope impact:** none on architecture. Doc-only carryover PR (zero code/test changes). The two surprises (bind-mount-inode + UFW+Docker bypass) result in **runbook updates** in `deploy/api/README.md` to prevent recurrence — net negative incremental work for future deploys. **Closes:** Week 5 verification gate box 1 fully (external-curl half via two-surface smoke); the Day 17 PR's "(Pending) Day 17 carryover" test plan item set. **Introduces:** the two runbook patches (bind-mount-inode warning in Step 2; UFW+Docker bypass + `docker compose stop` corrective in Week-5-gate-box-3 subsection) — these are now part of the canonical runbook.

Locked constants: `BOOTSTRAP_ALPHA = Decimal("0")` + `BOOTSTRAP_BETA = Decimal("0")` (the §2.14 zero-slippage prior); `MIN_OBSERVATIONS_FOR_OLS = 30` (per the §2.14 "first real calibration after 30 days of Phase 1 LIVE fills" lock — applied per market); `BPS_PER_UNIT = Decimal("10000")` (1 bp = 0.01%). Two StrEnums: `CalibrationFitMethod` (zero_prior / ols; plan-level label, with per-market label implicit in `MarketCoefficient.r_squared`) + `CalibrationTrigger` (bootstrap / scheduled_monthly / scheduled_quarterly / manual; mirrors §3.12 CHECK). `FillObservation` dataclass: market + order_size_contracts + adv_30d_contracts + expected_price + realized_price + direction (buy / sell) + fill_at_utc — Decimal + tz-aware enforced at boundary via `_validate_fill`. `MarketCoefficient` (alpha + beta + n_obs + r_squared|None — `r_squared=None` for zero-prior fall-back AND for both degenerate OLS branches: Sxx=0 collapses to constant intercept (`alpha=ybar, beta=0`), Syy=0 returns the OLS estimates but with `r_squared=None` since the response has no variance to explain). `PendingAuditEvent` (event_type Literal + payload dict — same shape as `services.reconciliation.recon.PendingAuditEvent` and `services.risk.sizing.PendingAuditEvent`; one-place flush helper for the future Week 4 dispatcher). `CalibrationPlan` (new_version_id UUID + version_no int + fit_method + trigger + per_market_coefficients Mapping[str, MarketCoefficient] + data_window_start_utc + data_window_end_utc + fill_count + parent_version_id UUID|None + becomes_head bool + audit_event PendingAuditEvent) — all frozen + slots. `CalibrationError(ValueError)` for caller-bug rejections. Slippage sign convention locked: `+bps = trader paid worse than expected mid` (buy above expected → +; sell below expected → +). OLS arithmetic Decimal-only (no float promotion): `Sxx = sum((x-xbar)**2)`, `Sxy = sum((x-xbar)*(y-ybar))`, `Syy = sum((y-ybar)**2)`, `beta = Sxy / Sxx`, `alpha = ybar - beta * xbar`, `r_squared = 1 - sum((y-yhat)**2) / Syy`. Plan-level `fit_method = OLS` when at least one market in `per_market_coefficients` had `n_obs >= MIN_OBSERVATIONS_FOR_OLS` (i.e. ran through the estimator code path even when degenerate-data branches collapsed it); else `ZERO_PRIOR`.
